# Observation checklist

Generated by `cargo xtask observe` from `spec/inventory/*.toml`; do not edit. `cargo xtask ci` fails when it is out of date.

Clean-room: observe the reference app on our own synthetic fonts; never copy its assets or text.

## What is listed

Every inventory row that watching the running reference app could settle:

- rows with `guesses`: choices of ours where no source says what the reference does;
- rows whose `status` is `memory` (recalled), and rows whose `status` is `inferred` (our design) that point at a handbook chapter;
- rows with `observe` steps: one concrete thing to do in the app and what to look for.

Left out: rows already `observed`, rows with `own = true` (no counterpart in the reference app), and rows that other tools settle (command-line rows, quality checks, binary tables).

Rows of built milestones come first, because their guesses are behaviour FontCraft already has. Within a milestone group the rows are sorted by the place in the app where the check happens, so one sitting can stay in one window. `live` after a row's status marks a command FontCraft registers today, which can be tried side by side.

## How to record a result

Edit the row in `spec/inventory/*.toml`:

1. Write what the app did and the date in the row's `notes`.
2. Remove each guess the observation settles. If the app does something else, change FontCraft's behaviour and the row's `summary` to match.
3. Remove the `observe` steps you carried out.
4. When nothing about the row is left open, set `status = "observed"`.
5. Run `cargo xtask guesses` and `cargo xtask observe` and commit the regenerated files.

The checkboxes are for a printed or copied list; a settled row drops out of this file when it is regenerated.

To add a check, append a step to the row's `observe` list: what to do, and what to look for.

## Built milestones (M0 to M6) (471)

### Edit View: drawing, guides, layers and the Palette (99)

- [ ] `dialog.fieldKeys` (behavior, M3, inferred): In a dialog's number field, Return takes the typed value, Escape drops…
  - Guess: Dialog number fields follow the Info box's editing and arrow-key rules (infoBox.fieldEditing, infoBox.arrowKeyStepping); the handbook documents them for the Info box only.
- [ ] `edit.cut` (M3, inferred, live): Cut
  - Guess: What Cut puts on the clipboard (our JSON text with an SVG flavour) is our format; the reference's clipboard content is not documented.
- [ ] `edit.copy` (M3, inferred, live): Copy
  - Guess: The clipboard text is our JSON format with an SVG flavour; what the reference puts on the clipboard is not documented.
- [ ] `edit.paste` (M3, inferred, live): Paste
  - Guess: Paste reads our JSON clipboard format or SVG; which other clipboard data the reference accepts is not documented.
- [ ] `tools.sharedButtonCycling` (behavior, M3, handbook): Tools that share one toolbar button (Select/Lasso, the two Primitives modes, Erase/Knife)…
  - Guess: Holding a shared toolbar button for 0.4 s opens its menu; the reference's hold time is not documented.
- [ ] `nodes.smoothAndCornerTypes` (behavior, M3, handbook): Every node is either smooth or corner
  - Guess: Selected nodes, handles and segments are drawn in orange; no source names the reference's selection colour.
- [ ] `selection.marqueeSelectsNodesAndHandles` (behavior, M3, handbook): A marquee picks up both nodes and handles that fall inside it;…
  - Guess: A marquee selects the handles inside it whatever their node (03a question 7).
- [ ] `selection.segmentCountsAsSelected` (behavior, M3, handbook): A segment is treated as selected when both of its end nodes…
  - Guess: Clicking a segment selects its two end nodes (03a question 7).
- [ ] `move.nodesCarryHandles` (behavior, M3, handbook): Moving a node moves the handles attached to it by the same…
  - Guess: Alt with an arrow key moves only the selected points (03a question 1).
- [ ] `move.nudge` (behavior, M3, handbook): Nudging (Ctrl+Option while moving nodes, or Option pressed after a drag has…
  - Guess: Ctrl+Alt with an arrow key nudges, by the arrow increments 1, 10 and 100 (03a question 2).
- [ ] `nodes.metricLineHighlight` (behavior, M3, handbook): A beige diamond is drawn behind any node that sits precisely on…
  - Guess: "Vertical metric lines" are read as the master's height lines: ascender, cap height, x-height, baseline, descender and any other metric but the italic angle.
- [ ] `infoBox.selectionGeometryFields` (behavior, M3, handbook): With several nodes selected the Info box shows X, Y, width and…
  - Guess: The width and height lock starts open; the handbook gives no default.
- [ ] `duplicate.optionDrag` (behavior, M3, handbook): Dragging a selection with Option held leaves the original in place and…
  - Guess: Alt held at mouse-down on a selection drags a copy, and Alt pressed after the drag started switches to nudge (03a question 1).
- [ ] `stroke.joinsAndCaps` (behavior, M3, handbook): A stroke has one join style out of four, and open paths…
  - Guess: Every join and cap shape is our construction on offset curves (docs/spec/03a § 17): miter up to 4 half widths, then bevel.
  - Guess: Round full is round on the outer side and fills the inner corner with an arc of half the stroke width; round inset is a round cap that ends at the end node.
  - Guess: The axis-aligned cap cuts through the middle of the band at the end node and is a butt end when the path ends within about 6° of a forced axis.
- [ ] `compositing.shapesCombineInOrder` (behavior, M3, handbook): The final outline of a layer is the composite of its shapes…
  - Guess: Export applies strokes and masks before overlap removal, so a glyph whose layer has a stroke or a mask is compiled as contours only; the handbook does not say how the reference exports them.
- [ ] `snapping.toNodesAndHandles` (behavior, M3, handbook): Any dragged node or other object snaps to the nodes and handles…
  - Guess: The snap distance is 4 screen points, with the priority nodes, handles, guides, metric lines (03a question 28).
  - Guess: Ctrl suspends snapping as well as magnetic guides.
- [ ] `guides.knobAndSelection` (behavior, M3, handbook): A guide is selected by clicking anywhere on it and shows a…
  - Guess: A drag on the guide away from its knob turns it about the knob, the pressed side following the pointer (03a question 27).
  - Guess: A double-click on the guide away from its knob opens dialog.guideProperties, our addition.
- [ ] `guides.angleLock` (behavior, M3, handbook): Closing the lock next to a guide's angle in the Info box…
  - Guess: A drag on an angle-locked guide away from its knob moves the guide, as a knob drag does (03a question 27).
- [ ] `guides.sidebearingReference` (behavior, M3, handbook): A guide's horizontal position is measured from the left sidebearing by default
  - Guess: Left keeps the guide's x, right moves it with the width, and both keeps its fraction of the width (03a question 27).
- [ ] `view.showNextTab` (M3, handbook, live): Show Next Tab
  - Guess: After the last tab comes Font View, and before Font View the last tab.
- [ ] `view.closeTab` (M3, handbook, live): Close Tab
  - Guess: The tab to the left of a closed showing tab shows next.
  - Guess: The last 32 closed tabs are kept for Reopen Last Closed Tab.
- [ ] `selection.selectNext` (M3, handbook, live): Select Next
  - Guess: Tab steps through nodes in path order, then the next path, then anchors, then guides, then back to the start (03a question 8).
- [ ] `path.toggleNodeType` (M3, handbook, live): Toggle Node Type
  - Guess: Turning a corner smooth adds missing handles a third of the way along the segment and turns handles that are not collinear to the bisector of their directions, keeping their lengths (03a question 9).
- [ ] `path.tidyUpPaths` (M3, handbook, live): Tidy up Paths
  - Guess: Points within 0.01 units count as on a line or as one point.
  - Guess: A node counts as smooth when a curve meets it and its two directions are within 1° (03a question 9).
- [ ] `infoBox.setSizeLock` (M3, inferred, live): Lock Info Box Proportions
  - Guess: The lock starts open; the handbook gives no default.
- [ ] `path.alignSelection` (M3, handbook, live): Align Selection
  - Guess: When width equals height the selection aligns on one X, and the transformation origin's column or row picks min, centre or max (03a question 13).
- [ ] `edit.delete` (M3, handbook, live): Delete
  - Guess: The refitted segment keeps its end nodes and the directions it leaves and enters them in, and fits the two handle lengths by least squares over the removed segments (03a question 10).
  - Guess: With only handles selected the segment becomes a line; with every node of a path selected the path is removed.
  - Guess: In text mode, Delete run from a menu or the control channel removes the selected text, else the item after the cursor, as the Delete key does; the Edit menu of the running reference app in text mode settles it.
- [ ] `edit.deleteInAllMasters` (M3, handbook, live): Delete in All Masters
  - Guess: Nodes are matched across masters by address; a layer whose structure differs is skipped and listed (03a question 11).
  - Guess: In text mode it is refused with a message, so it never deletes nodes the person cannot see; the Edit menu of the running reference app in text mode settles it.
- [ ] `path.openCorner` (M3, handbook, live): Open Corner
  - Guess: A master with stems of one direction only uses that stem for both directions, and a master without stems uses an overlap of 4% of the em.
  - Guess: Each end node moves along its own segment's direction, so the handles keep their directions.
  - Guess: Corners whose two directions are within 1° of straight or reversed are left alone.
- [ ] `path.reconnectNodes` (M3, handbook, live): Reconnect Nodes
  - Guess: Pairs go nearest first, ties to the lower address (03a question 15).
  - Guess: An open-corner pair becomes one node where the incoming direction of the first and the outgoing direction of the second meet; any other pair is rewired with straight segments.
- [ ] `path.makeNodeFirst` (M3, handbook, live): Make Node First
  - Guess: The context menu offers it only when every selected on-curve node lies on a closed path.
- [ ] `path.reverseContoursForAllMasters` (M3, handbook, live): Reverse Contours for All Masters
  - Guess: The label "Reverse Contours for All Masters" is ours, and paths are matched across masters by address (03a questions 11 and 18).
- [ ] `path.correctPathDirection` (M3, handbook, live): Correct Path Direction
  - Guess: A path is inside another when that one is larger, holds its bounding box and holds most of its sample points; paths that only overlap are both outer.
  - Guess: Shapes are sorted by the bottom-left corner of their bounds, and the start node is the lowest, then leftmost, on-curve node (03a question 16).
- [ ] `path.addExtremes` (M3, handbook, live): Add Extremes
  - Guess: A node within 2 units and 2° of an extreme keeps its place, and its handles within 2° of the axis are made exactly horizontal or vertical (03a question 17).
  - Guess: An extreme is skipped when the chord from it to the end of its segment, or to the extreme before it, is shorter than 3 units.
- [ ] `path.forceExtremes` (M3, handbook, live): Force Extremes
  - Guess: Force Extremes adds every extreme that rounds to a new point, however short the segment it leaves.
- [ ] `path.addInflections` (M3, inferred, live): Add Inflections
  - Guess: An inflection within 1 unit of a segment end gets no node, the distance under which check.outlines.inflections ignores one.
- [ ] `path.focusOnSelectedPath` (M3, handbook, live): Focus on Selected Path
  - Guess: Path focus belongs to the tab and is cleared when the tab's edited glyph changes (03a question 19).
- [ ] `glyph.toggleLocked` (M3, handbook, live): Locked
  - Guess: Locks are stored in the file, and a glyph lock refuses every change to the glyph's own data (layers, outlines, anchors, guides, width, metrics keys) but not kerning (03a question 20).
- [ ] `path.setStroke` (M3, inferred, live): Set Stroke
  - Guess: The axis-aligned cap is stored as cap code 4; not confirmed against the reference's files.
  - Guess: A forced cap axis is kept in the native key `lineCapAxis`, which .glyphs files do not hold, so saving as .glyphs loses it (a known loss).
  - Guess: A UFO keeps no stroke or mask attributes, so saving as UFO loses strokes and masks (a known loss).
  - Guess: Stroke positions and mask values keep the stored form of the document's .glyphs version, so converting a document between versions 3 and 4 changes where strokes sit and what masks do (a known loss until the reference's conversion is observed).
- [ ] `path.setMask` (M3, handbook, live): Mask
  - Guess: Only paths take the mask flag, though a mask cuts components below it too (03a question 23).
- [ ] `guide.setProperties` (M3, inferred, live): Set Properties
  - Guess: Both sidebearings are written as the guide's `alignment = center` and the right one as `right`; our reading of the format, not confirmed.
- [ ] `guide.remove` (M3, inferred, live): Remove
  - Guess: Delete removes a selected guide; the chapter does not say how the reference deletes one (03a question 27).
- [ ] `dialog.primitiveMeasurements` (panel, M3, handbook): Primitive Measurements
  - Guess: The width and height fields run from 1 to 2,000,000 units and open at 100.
  - Guess: The click is the shape's bottom-left corner (03a question 5).
- [ ] `editView.toolbar` (panel, M3, handbook): Edit View Toolbar
  - Guess: A secondary click on a shared button also opens its menu; no source shows the reference doing so.
- [ ] `dialog.shapeOrder` (panel, M3, handbook): Shape Order
  - Guess: The first row is the bottom shape (shape 0, drawn first).
  - Guess: Rows read `<index>: Path (<n> nodes)`, `<index>: Component <name>` or `<index>: Image`; what the reference's rows show and which end is the top are not observed (03a open question 24).
- [ ] `dialog.guideProperties` (panel, M3, inferred): Guide Properties
  - Guess: The dialog is our own form over guide.setProperties; the reference edits a guide in the Info box.
- [ ] `draw` (tool, M3, handbook): Draw
  - Guess: A click on a segment places the new node on the segment and leaves the clicked path alone (03a question 3).
  - Guess: Shift+click on a segment inserts at an extremum or inflection even while drawing.
  - Guess: A click on an open end continues that path, and while drawing joins the two paths.
  - Guess: A missing handle of a curved segment lies a third of the way from its node to the other handle.
  - Guess: Shift keeps nodes and handles to 45° steps (03a question 4).
  - Guess: Escape or another tool ends the path.
- [ ] `pencil` (tool, M3, handbook): Pencil
  - Guess: The stroke is fitted with cubic curves within one screen point, so it stays node-heavy.
  - Guess: A stroke that ends on its start, within the hit tolerance, gives a closed path.
- [ ] `select` (tool, M3, handbook): Select
  - Guess: The canvas context menu's sections, item targets and what a right-click selects first are our design (docs/spec/03a § 6).
- [ ] `background.snapping` (behavior, M3, handbook): While the background is displayed, its nodes and outlines are snapping targets…
  - Guess: The background's nodes, then its handles, are snap targets after the edited layer's nodes and handles and before guides; its outlines between nodes are not targets.
- [ ] `infoBox.setReferencePoint` (M3, handbook, live): Set Info Box Reference Point
  - Guess: The reference point starts at bottom left; the handbook gives no default.
- [ ] `view.zoomIn` (M3, handbook, live): Zoom In
  - Guess: One step multiplies the scale by √2, and the zoom value stays between 50 and 64,000 (03b question 8).
- [ ] `view.zoomToActiveLayer` (M3, handbook, live): Zoom to Active Layer
  - Guess: The descender-to-ascender span, or the advance width when that is the tighter limit, fills the window less 8% on each side.
- [ ] `view.zoomToRect` (M3, inferred, live): Zoom to Rectangle
  - Guess: The rectangle fills the window less 8% on each side, the margin of Zoom to Active Layer.
- [ ] `path.editBackground` (M3, handbook, live): Edit Background
  - Guess: The edited side belongs to the tab and stays when another glyph, layer or master is edited.
  - Guess: Audit markers are not drawn on the background.
- [ ] `path.selectionToBackground` (M3, handbook, live): Selection to Background
  - Guess: A selected point or segment copies its whole path, and the other side's guides, hints, annotations and image stay; the handbook does not say what is replaced.
- [ ] `path.addSelectionToBackground` (M3, handbook, live): Add Selection to Background
  - Guess: A copied anchor replaces the background's anchor of the same name.
- [ ] `path.swapWithBackground` (M3, handbook, live): Swap with Background
  - Guess: Swap exchanges shapes, anchors, guides, hints and annotations but not the placed image, and removes a background the swap leaves empty.
- [ ] `path.clearBackground` (M3, handbook, live): Clear Background
  - Guess: Clear Background is its own Path menu item and acts on the active layer of the targeted glyphs, image included (03b question 2).
- [ ] `path.assignBackground` (M3, handbook, live): Assign Background
  - Guess: Glyphs are matched by name and masters by name, then by position; a source with fewer masters gives its first (03b question 3).
- [ ] `measure.ruler` (M3, inferred, live): Measure Along Line
  - Guess: Crossings follow the nonzero winding rule, and a line that only touches a contour does not cross it; the handbook does not say how the reference merges overlapping contours.
  - Guess: Components are measured like paths, and the ruler stays until the next click or tool change (03b question 10).
- [ ] `annotation.add` (M3, inferred, live): Add Annotation
  - Guess: New annotations get a text box 200 units wide, an arrow at 45° and a circle 100 units across; the handbook gives no defaults (03b question 11).
- [ ] `measurement` (tool, M3, handbook): Measurement
  - Guess: The ruler's ends snap unless Ctrl is held, and Shift keeps it horizontal or vertical, whichever is nearer.
  - Guess: The ruler stays until the next press, Escape or a tool change (03b question 9).
  - Guess: G with any modifiers adds the measurement guide.
- [ ] `annotation` (tool, M3, handbook): Annotation
  - Guess: Each annotation sub-tool is a tool of its own, and A returns to the sub-tool used last (Annotation Selection before any).
  - Guess: Handles show and work on selected annotations only.
- [ ] `dimensions.perMaster` (behavior, M3, handbook): Dimensions values are kept separately for every master, so switching master switches…
  - Guess: Values are stored under `GSDimensionPlugin.Dimensions` in the font's user data, one dictionary per master id, the key the corpus files use (Palette question 1).
- [ ] `fitCurve.percentRange` (behavior, M3, handbook): Handle length is expressed as a percentage from 1 to 100
  - Guess: Without the `Fit Curve Panel Settings` parameter the range is 48 to 74 %, the handbook's example, and values beyond the range are clamped to it (Palette question 2).
  - Observe: In a new font without the Fit Curve Panel Settings parameter, open the Palette's Fit Curve section and read the two percentage fields.
- [ ] `transform.oppositeButtonsExact` (behavior, M3, handbook): The buttons on either side of a value field are exact inverses…
  - Guess: Opposite buttons are exact for mirroring and quarter turns, through exact sines and cosines and grid rounding; other angles are undone only up to grid rounding (Palette question 5).
- [ ] `dimensions.getValues` (M3, inferred, live): Get Dimensions
  - Guess: The field keys per script come from the corpus files (Latin: HH, HV, OH, OV, nV, nd, oH, oV, tH; Arabic: arAlef, arBar); the reference does not document them.
- [ ] `fitCurve.applyStep` (M3, handbook, live): Fit Curve
  - Observe: Select a curve segment, press Ctrl+Alt+1, then Cmd+Ctrl+Alt+1, and note which of the two changes the handle lengths; then read the Fit Curve entries in Settings › Shortcuts.
- [ ] `fitCurve.setHandleLength` (M3, inferred, live): Set Handle Length
  - Guess: A handle's percentage is its length over the distance from its node to the intersection of the two handles, or the chord when the handles are parallel (Palette question 2).
- [ ] `fitCurve.increase` (M3, inferred, live): Increase Handle Length
  - Guess: Plus moves to the next of the eight steps above the segment's mean percentage; the handbook gives no increment.
- [ ] `fitCurve.decrease` (M3, inferred, live): Decrease Handle Length
  - Guess: Minus moves to the step below the segment's mean percentage; the handbook gives no increment.
- [ ] `fitCurve.balanceHandles` (M3, inferred, live): Balance Handles
  - Guess: Balance sets both handles to their mean percentage; the handbook gives no rule.
- [ ] `layers.toggleVisibility` (M3, handbook, live): Show / Hide Layer
  - Guess: Visibility is stored with the layer and saved (Palette question 3).
- [ ] `layers.addBackupLayer` (M3, handbook, live): New Backup Layer
  - Guess: Plus copies the active layer, and the backup is named by the date and time in UTC (Palette question 3).
- [ ] `layers.useAsMaster` (M3, handbook, live): Use as Master
  - Guess: Use as Master takes backup layers only (Palette question 4).
- [ ] `layers.list` (M3, inferred, live): List Layers
  - Guess: Rows are ordered by master in master order, each master layer followed by that master's other layers in file order.
- [ ] `transform.setOrigin` (M3, handbook, live): Set Transformation Origin
  - Guess: The metrics-point origin sits at half the advance width for every operation, not only mirroring (Palette question 5).
- [ ] `transform.slantLeft` (M3, handbook, live): Slant Left
  - Guess: Slant is a shear by the tangent of the angle about the origin (Palette question 5).
- [ ] `transform.slantDown` (M3, inferred, live): Slant Down
  - Guess: What lies right of the transformation origin moves down; the handbook does not say which side moves.
- [ ] `transform.slantUp` (M3, inferred, live): Slant Up
  - Guess: What lies right of the transformation origin moves up; the handbook does not say which side moves.
- [ ] `transform.alignLeft` (M3, handbook, live): Align Left
  - Guess: Aligning a single object or point changes nothing and says so (Palette question 7).
- [ ] `transform.booleanUnion` (M3, handbook, live): Union
  - Guess: Open paths stay as they are, unlike Remove Overlap, and components are not changed (Palette question 8).
- [ ] `transform.booleanSubtract` (M3, handbook, live): Subtract
  - Guess: The frontmost path is the last closed path in shape order (Palette question 8).
- [ ] `transform.booleanIntersect` (M3, handbook, live): Intersect
  - Guess: The frontmost path is the last closed path in shape order (Palette question 8).
- [ ] `transform.getSettings` (M3, inferred, live): Get Transformation Settings
  - Guess: The starting values are ours: the box centre, 110 % with the lock closed, 90° and 10°.
- [ ] `transform.setSettings` (M3, inferred, live): Set Transformation Values
  - Guess: The defaults, scale 110 % with the lock closed, rotation 90° and slant 10°, are ours; the tutorial video named in palette.transformations shows the second scale field greyed, which suggests a closed lock.
  - Guess: The limits are ours: a scale is non-zero and within ±1000000 %, a rotation within ±3600°, a slant strictly between -90° and 90°.
- [ ] `guides.globalScopeFilter` (behavior, M4, handbook): A global guide appears on all glyphs by default
  - Guess: A filter that does not read as a predicate leaves the guide shown in every glyph. Settled by opening a file whose guide filter the reference cannot read.
- [ ] `view.editSelectedGlyphs` (M4, handbook, live): Edit Selected Glyphs
  - Guess: An open tab that holds exactly these glyphs is shown instead of a new tab.
- [ ] `infoBox.showsColorLabels` (behavior, M4, handbook): The Info box shows the glyph colour label on its left half…
  - Guess: The split square sits at the left of the Info box and is drawn only when the glyph or the active layer has a colour label; the Info box itself shows only while something is selected. Settled by observation.
- [ ] `editView.contextMenu` (panel, M4, handbook): Edit View canvas context menu
  - Guess: The swatch row opens the glyph section of the menu, under a Glyph Color caption (Layer Color while Alt is held). Settled by observation.
  - Guess: The glyph section offers Exports, Locked and Show Glyph in Font View, and the menu ends with Copy Glyph Names, as the Glyphs 3 video lists them; the other entries of that list follow with their milestones. Settled by observation.
- [ ] `view.showMeasurementLine` (M5, handbook, live): Show Measurement Line
  - Guess: The numbers sit just above the line, each centred between the advance edge and the crossing it measures, and a glyph narrower than 24 screen points gets none; watching the reference app with a known glyph would settle it.
  - Guess: In top-to-bottom text the line is not drawn; the handbook only describes horizontal text.
- [ ] `view.setMeasurementLineHeight` (M5, handbook, live): Set Measurement Line Height
  - Guess: The text-mode measurement line height is kept per tab and starts at half the x-height of the tab master; no source gives the default or where it is kept.
  - Guess: A click or drag sets a whole-unit height, and Cmd+Ctrl+Alt only moves the line while it is shown (otherwise the click is an ordinary one); the reference app would settle both.
- [ ] `infoBox.glyph` (panel, M5, handbook): Info Box (glyph, horizontal layout)
  - Guess: With several glyphs selected in the text, the Info box describes the glyph after the cursor only, and the Unicode value is shown, not edited.
  - Guess: At each outer edge the kern field sits on the sidebearing row and the group field with its lock on the width row; a screenshot of the reference app's Info box in text mode would settle it.
  - Guess: The preceding glyph's box sits beside the glyph box, on the side where the preceding glyph is drawn; the reference app with the Text tool would settle it.
- [ ] `align.anchorToPoints` (behavior, M6, handbook): When the selection is a single anchor plus a single path point,…
  - Guess: With two points the anchor keeps its height and moves horizontally onto the line at the italic angle through the points' midpoint, and a locked anchor refuses it; aligning an anchor between two nodes of different heights on an italic master in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `align.componentOriginPoint` (behavior, M6, handbook): A component's origin point is the position of an anchor named origin…
  - Guess: The left sidebearing line is x = 0 of the base glyph, and on a slanted master it is slanted about half the x-height, so without an origin anchor the origin point is the base glyph's (0, 0) on every master; aligning a component on an italic master in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `component.setTransform` (M6, handbook, live): Set Component Transform
  - Guess: The command refuses a position edit on an aligned component (only `y` on one aligned horizontally) and any edit on a locked component, and accepts slants within ±89°, rotations within ±360° and scales from −10,000 % to 10,000 % except 0; typing such values into the Info box of the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `view.showAnchors` (M6, handbook, live): Show Anchors
  - Guess: Show Anchors is on by default; the View menu of a fresh install of the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.swapBaseKeepsPlacement` (behavior, M6, inferred): Changing the glyph a component points to (component.setBaseGlyph, defined in the Edit…
  - Guess: Swapping the base glyph keeps the component's slot in the shape order, its transform, its alignment setting and its stored attachment anchor; swapping a component in the reference app settles it.

### Edit View: text mode and preview (32)

- [ ] `text.lineWidth` (behavior, M5, handbook): Text in Edit View wraps at a preset maximum line width, which…
  - Guess: Lines wrap at 10,000 units unless the setting says otherwise; no source gives the default.
  - Guess: A line that overflows breaks after its last space, else before the glyph that overflows, and a space may hang past the width; wrapped text in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: In top-to-bottom text the lines are columns, placed from right to left one line height apart; a vertical tab in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `text.currentGlyphAfterCursor` (behavior, M5, handbook): In text mode the glyph immediately after the text cursor is the…
  - Guess: At the end of the text or of a line the current glyph is the glyph before the cursor, and next to a placeholder or a missing character it stays what it was; moving the cursor there in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `text.clipboard` (behavior, M5, handbook): Text can be copied out of Edit View and pasted into it…
  - Guess: A glyph without a character is copied as `/name`, as display strings write it, and pasted text reads `/name` as that glyph, so a copy pastes back unchanged; copying unencoded glyphs from the reference app into a plain-text editor settles it.
- [ ] `text.displayStrings` (behavior, M5, docs): The texts of the open Edit View tabs are stored in the…
  - Guess: Changing a tab's text is not an undo step and does not mark the document modified; no source says, and the window's edited mark after typing in a tab of the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A `/` with an empty name is the slash character, and a glyph is written as the character it was typed as only while the font still maps that character to it; saving such a string in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A `/name` the font lacks and a character no glyph encodes are kept in the text and take half the em with nothing drawn; opening such a string in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A stored string over the 10,000-item tab limit opens cut to its first 10,000 items, with a warning in the log and the status bar, and a string that cannot be read (a `/name` longer than a glyph name may be) opens no tab; a file from the reference app with such a string settles it.
  - Guess: Opening a document adds its tabs after the open ones and leaves Font View showing; opening a file with display strings in the reference app settles which tab shows.
- [ ] `text.findUnicodeMode` (behavior, M5, handbook): In Unicode mode the glyph search compares the query with the hexadecimal…
  - Guess: A Unicode term matches when a code point's hexadecimal form contains it, so `228` finds U+228E; whether the reference needs the whole code point would settle it.
- [ ] `textPreview.fallbackFont` (behavior, M5, handbook): Characters the font does not cover are drawn in a system fallback…
  - Guess: Characters the font lacks are drawn in the interface font of the app.
  - Guess: Each character the font lacks takes half an em, the advance the Edit View layout gives a missing character, whatever its width in the interface font.
- [ ] `preview.singleLine` (behavior, M5, handbook): The Preview Area and Preview Panel put the complete text of the…
  - Guess: On the preview line a line break takes no room; a tab of several lines in the reference app's Preview Area settles it.
  - Guess: Vertical text previews on a horizontal line, and right-to-left text from the right; the reference app with a vertical tab settles it.
- [ ] `editView.toggleTextMode` (M5, handbook, live): Toggle Text Mode
  - Guess: Leaving text mode makes the Select tool active, and returning puts the text cursor before the glyph that was edited (at the end of the text, when the last glyph was edited, the cursor stays at the end); no source names either, and the toolbar after Escape, in the running app or a video, settles it.
- [ ] `editView.insertText` (M5, handbook, live): Insert Text
  - Guess: A `/name` escape ends at the next slash, space or line break, and one space after it is consumed; the corpus files show the escape but not how it ends.
- [ ] `edit.find` (M5, handbook, live): Find…
  - Guess: The pop-up opens with an empty search field each time; whether the reference keeps the last query would settle it.
  - Guess: Up and Down in the search field move the selection to the result above or below; the handbook names only clicks.
  - Guess: The selected results go into the text in list order, not in the order they were picked.
- [ ] `editView.previousGlyph` (M5, handbook, live): Previous Glyph
  - Guess: At the first glyph of the order Previous Glyph does nothing (no wrap), and with several glyphs selected in the text it replaces only the current glyph.
- [ ] `editView.nextGlyph` (M5, handbook, live): Next Glyph
  - Guess: At the last glyph of the order Next Glyph does nothing (no wrap).
- [ ] `editView.previousVisibleGlyph` (M5, handbook, live): Previous Glyph in Font View
  - Guess: For a current glyph that Font View does not list, Previous Glyph in Font View takes the last glyph listed; stepping from such a glyph in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `editView.nextVisibleGlyph` (M5, handbook, live): Next Glyph in Font View
  - Guess: For a current glyph that Font View does not list, Next Glyph in Font View takes the first glyph listed; stepping from such a glyph in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `edit.addPlaceholder` (M5, handbook, live): Add Placeholder
  - Guess: A placeholder is written `/Placeholder` in display strings and copied to the plain-text clipboard as the text of the current glyph; no source shows either.
  - Guess: A placeholder goes in place of the selection, as a typed character does; adding one with text selected in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `editView.setWritingDirection` (M5, handbook, live): Writing Direction
  - Guess: Vertical text in text mode draws no horizontal metric lines, since every column has its own; a vertical tab in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `textPreview.setStyle` (M5, handbook, live): Preview Font Style
  - Guess: Until instances are interpolated (M9) the style list holds the masters and the instances that sit at a master location.
- [ ] `textPreview.setFontSize` (M5, handbook, live): Preview Font Size
  - Guess: A new preview draws at 36 points, and sizes from 4 to 1,000 points are allowed; the reference app's field would settle both.
- [ ] `textPreview.togglePinned` (M5, handbook, live): Pin Text Preview
  - Guess: The Text Preview is a window inside the app window; pinned, it stays in front of the other windows there, unpinned it goes behind the one clicked last. A separate pinned window in the reference app is the model.
- [ ] `textPreview.setLayoutOrientation` (M5, handbook, live): Layout Orientation
  - Guess: Vertical text runs in columns from right to left, with the vertical widths and origins Edit View uses; the reference app on our own vertical font would settle it.
- [ ] `textPreview.setWritingDirection` (M5, handbook, live): Writing Direction
  - Guess: The default direction of the text comes from its first letter: right to left for Hebrew, Arabic and the other right-to-left scripts, else left to right. The reference follows the Unicode bidirectional algorithm; a mixed text in the reference app would settle how far we differ.
  - Guess: A selected range with the other direction is an embedding: its base direction changes, one level inside the text's, and its runs are ordered by it; it keeps its direction while text is typed around it; directions do nothing in vertical text. The reference app on a mixed text would settle it.
  - Guess: Digits, Arabic-Indic digits included, form left-to-right runs; a neutral between two runs of the same direction takes that direction, else the base direction of the text or its range; a combining mark follows the character before it. Full UAX #9 at M8 would settle the cases where this differs.
  - Guess: Digits count as left to right, so "12 34" in right-to-left text shows as "12 34" where full Unicode bidi gives "34 12"; M8's UAX #9 would settle it.
  - Guess: Line breaking measures a trailing blank with the kern it has before its level is reset to the paragraph's, so a line can break up to one kern early; full bidi in M8 would settle how the reference measures it.
- [ ] `textPreview.find` (M5, handbook, live): Find…
  - Guess: The search options are Ignore Case (on at first) and Contains, Starts With or Full Word, as the system find bar of the reference offers them; matches do not overlap.
  - Guess: Replace changes the current match, All changes every match; a replacement that would take the text past 1,000,000 characters is refused.
- [ ] `edit.selectSampleText` (M5, handbook, live): Select Sample Text…
  - Guess: Choosing a sample string replaces the whole text of the tab; the handbook says the string goes into the text without saying where.
- [ ] `edit.selectNextSampleString` (M5, handbook, live): Select Next Sample String
  - Guess: Next and Previous wrap around at the ends of the list.
  - Guess: Next and Previous step through the strings of all groups as one list, from the string chosen last in any tab; with none chosen yet, Next takes the first string and Previous the last.
- [ ] `view.togglePreviewArea` (M5, handbook, live): Preview
  - Guess: Each tab opens and closes its own Preview Area, while all tabs share one height; a second tab in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The area is 140 points tall until its separator is dragged; the reference app's first height settles it.
- [ ] `window.previewPanel` (M5, handbook, live): Preview Panel
  - Guess: The Preview Panel keeps Invert, Flip, Blur, centring and offset of its own, apart from the Preview Area; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `preview.flip` (M5, handbook, live): Flip
  - Guess: Flip turns the strip half a circle, as a printed proof turned upside down, rather than mirroring it top to bottom; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `preview.setBlur` (M5, handbook, live): Blur
  - Guess: The blur amount runs from 0 to 10 screen points of blur radius; the handbook gives no range or unit.
- [ ] `preview.alwaysCenterActiveGlyph` (M5, handbook, live): Always Center Active Glyph
  - Guess: Off, a line wider than the strip sits as near to centring the edited glyph as it can without leaving a gap at either edge; the reference app with a long text settles it.
- [ ] `window.textPreview` (panel, M5, handbook): Text Preview
  - Guess: The text is edited in a plain field above the area that draws it in the font, since FontCraft has no text field that draws in the font being edited; the field edits texts of up to 100,000 characters, longer ones change through Set Preview Text and Find and Replace. The reference field both edits and draws; a FontCraft field that draws in the font would settle it.
  - Guess: The style pop-up starts on the master of the showing Edit View tab and follows it until a style is picked; the reference app's first choice would settle it.
  - Guess: Find opens with Cmd+F while the pointer is over the window and with the window's Find button, because Edit › Find… with Cmd+F belongs to the Edit View Find command; a focused Text Preview in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `editView.sampleStrings` (panel, M5, handbook): Select Sample Text
  - Guess: Up and Down put the previous or next string into the text at once and keep the window open; a click closes it unless it is pinned; Return and Escape close it.
- [ ] `text` (tool, M5, handbook): Text
  - Guess: Return types a line break; the handbook allows several lines but does not name the key.
  - Guess: Up and Down move to the nearest place on the line above or below, and to the start or the end of the text from the first or the last line; in right-to-left text Left steps forward in the text; in top-to-bottom text Up and Down step along the column and Left and Right move between columns. Moving the cursor in such texts in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Backspace and Delete with Alt or Cmd do nothing (no word or line deletion); no source names them.

### Components and anchors (70)

- [ ] `export.underscoreGlyphsOmitted` (behavior, M2, inferred): Non-exporting glyphs, which underscore-named part glyphs are by default, are left out…
  - Guess: Components that use non-exporting glyphs are decomposed at export; our inference from the non-export rule.
- [ ] `edit.selectAll` (M3, handbook, live): Select All
  - Guess: In Font View it selects every glyph; the handbook describes edit mode only.
- [ ] `anchors.shapeShowsMetricPosition` (behavior, M6, handbook): The anchor marker is a diamond when the anchor sits precisely on…
  - Observe: Put an anchor on the x-height, one inside the baseline zone and one elsewhere, and note the three shapes; repeat with names that start with an underscore.
- [ ] `anchor.add` (M6, handbook, live): Add Anchor
  - Guess: When `name` is omitted the anchor is named new anchor, and a name already on the layer, given or not, gets underscores appended until it is unique, as Option-drag duplication does (anchors.uniqueNamesPerLayer); adding two anchors without renaming in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `anchor.rename` (M6, handbook, live): Rename
  - Guess: Renaming an anchor to a name already on the layer is refused with a message; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.setAnchors` (M6, handbook, live): Set Anchors
  - Guess: Set Anchors adds only the default anchors the layer lacks and leaves existing anchors where they are; Set Anchors on a glyph with a moved anchor in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The default anchors come from our table in `components::default_anchors` (M6.3): letters get top (cap height for uppercase; x-height for lowercase, or the ascender when the outline rises above the middle between the two; the outline's top without a case) and bottom (baseline), centred on the outline and slanted by the italic angle, plus ogonek for A E I O U a e i idotless o u, center for D H L O T d h l o t and topright for L O U d l o t u; marks above get _top at the x-height (cap height for .case) and top on the outline's top, marks below _bottom on the baseline and bottom on the outline's bottom, ogonekcomb _ogonek, horncomb and caroncomb.alt _topright; Han, Hangul, Kana, Yi and Bopomofo letters get none; the anchors stored in corpus sources saved by the reference app measure the table (M6 plan § 5).
  - Guess: A ligature named after its parts gets caret_1 to caret_(n-1) on the baseline, dividing the advance width equally; Set Anchors on f_f_l in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.resetAnchors` (M6, handbook, live): Reset Anchors
  - Guess: Reset Anchors keeps locked anchors, since a lock refuses every other change to an anchor; resetting a glyph with a locked anchor in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.resetAnchorsForAllMasters` (M6, handbook, live): Reset Anchors for All Masters
  - Guess: The shortcut is Cmd+Alt+Shift+U, Reset Anchors with Option added as for the other Option variants; the Glyph menu of the reference app with Option held settles it.
- [ ] `components.liveUpdateFromBaseGlyph` (behavior, M6, handbook): A component is a live reference to its base glyph: editing the…
  - Guess: Re-alignment after an edit touches only the masters whose layers the edit changed, and on other masters only the layers whose components are pinned to one of them (and the composites built on those layers), so stale stored positions on other masters stay as the file has them; editing a base on one master in the reference app and reading the other masters' composites settles it.
  - Guess: A locked composite is not re-aligned when its base changes, because a lock keeps the glyph's drawing; locking a composite in the reference app and editing its base settles it.
- [ ] `components.autoComposeOnAddGlyph` (behavior, M6, handbook): When a glyph that the glyph info database describes as a composite…
  - Guess: The Unicode decomposition is expanded fully (Aringacute is A, ringcomb, acutecomb), except that a letter with a horn stays whole when the font has it (Ohornacute is Ohorn, acutecomb); the composites stored in corpus sources saved by the reference app measure it (M6 plan § 5).
  - Guess: Our exceptions to Unicode: a name containing commaaccent takes commaaccentcomb where Unicode has the cedilla, gcommaaccent takes commaturnedabovecomb, and dcaron, lcaron, Lcaron and tcaron prefer caroncomb.alt; composing these in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Two marks in a row become their mark ligature (circumflexcomb_gravecomb, with the same suffix preferences) when the font has it; composing Acircumflexgrave in the reference app with the mark ligature present settles it.
  - Guess: A base of a decomposition takes the composite's suffix and is required (agrave.sc needs a.sc), while a ligature part takes the suffix only when the font has the suffixed glyph (f_f_l.ss01 from f.ss01 and l); composing suffixed glyphs in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A glyph whose parts are added by the same Add Glyphs text is built from them; adding eacute and acutecomb together in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Add to Font in the Glyph Info window composes as Add Glyphs does; adding a composite from that window in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.autoComposeNeedsAllParts` (behavior, M6, handbook): Automatic composition is all-or-nothing: if any one of the components the database…
  - Guess: The refusal names every missing part once: a mark by its plain name, a base by the name it needed (a.sc for agrave.sc); the message of the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.preferMarkWithSameSuffix` (behavior, M6, handbook): While composing a glyph automatically, a mark glyph carrying the same dot…
  - Guess: For an uppercase composite with a suffix the marks are tried as the suffixed mark, the .case mark, then the plain mark (Agrave.ss01: gravecomb.ss01, gravecomb.case, gravecomb); composing it in the reference app with all three present settles it.
- [ ] `components.preferNarrowMarksForIJ` (behavior, M6, handbook): Composites built on i or j (imacron, jcircumflex and the like) prefer…
  - Guess: A mark ending in .i is tried before one ending in .narrow, and both only on the dotless lowercase i and j, not on I or J; composing imacron in the reference app with both marks present settles it.
- [ ] `components.ijBuiltFromDotless` (behavior, M6, handbook): The default composition of i and j is the dotless letter (idotless,…
  - Guess: Composites lose the dot of i and j only before a mark above (Unicode combining class 230), so iogonek is i plus ogonekcomb; a suffixed i or j is composed only when the font has the suffixed dotless letter; composing iogonek and i.sc in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.recipeBasePlusMarks` (behavior, M6, handbook): Add Glyphs accepts the recipe form base+mark=glyph: names joined by plus signs…
  - Guess: A part whose glyph has category Mark is a mark and attaches to the base before it; any other part is a base; a recipe holds only glyph names, no spaces (spaces separate items in Add Glyphs) and any number of plus signs; typing recipes with code points or spaces into the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The first part of a recipe is a base even when its glyph is a mark (dotaccentcomb+dotaccentcomb=dieresiscomb sets the second on the first), and the category stored in the font wins over the database's; typing such a recipe in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.underscoreNameNotExported` (behavior, M6, handbook): A glyph whose name begins with an underscore defaults to non-exporting, so…
  - Guess: Renaming a glyph to or from an underscore name leaves its exports flag alone; renaming in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.tabCyclesComponents` (behavior, M6, handbook): With a component selected, Tab moves the selection to the next component…
  - Guess: Tab steps through the layer's nodes in path order, then the components in shape order, then the anchors, then the guides; Tab on a layer with components and anchors in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.infoBoxExtraControls` (behavior, M6, handbook): Beyond the fields listed for the component Info box in the Edit…
  - Guess: The arrow button sits at the top right of the component Info box, as the Measuring page says; the Components page says top left. A screenshot of the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.flipViaNegativeScaleOrMirror` (behavior, M6, inferred): A component is flipped with the Palette mirror buttons or by transforming…
  - Guess: The mirror buttons and a negative scale in the Info box give the same stored transform; mirroring a component in the reference app and saving settles it.
- [ ] `components.rubberBandIgnoresComponents` (behavior, M6, handbook): A selection rectangle dragged with the Select tool skips components; holding Option…
  - Guess: A rubber band with Option takes a component when an on-curve point of its outline lies inside, not only when the whole component does; dragging across half a component with Option in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Option in a rubber band both includes components and leaves handles out, since the handbook gives Option both meanings; dragging with Option over a path with handles and a component in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: With Option held, drag a selection rectangle over a path with handles and half of a component, and note what is selected.
- [ ] `components.doubleClickOpensBase` (behavior, M6, handbook): Double-clicking a component places its base glyph next to the composite in…
  - Guess: The base glyph goes into the tab text to the left of the composite, as the Info box section of the Measuring page says; the Components page says only next to it. Double-clicking a component in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.lockedCannotMove` (behavior, M6, handbook): A locked component rejects every move, from mouse, arrow keys or Info…
  - Guess: A lock blocks Info box position edits and the Palette's transformations as well as moves, and leaves the component's other settings editable; a locked component in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.placeholderEmptyBase` (behavior, M6, handbook): A component whose base glyph exists but has an empty layer is…
  - Guess: A placeholder is a crossed box the width of the base glyph's advance and half an em tall, labelled with the problem; seeing the three placeholders in the reference app settles their look.
  - Observe: Add a component of a glyph whose layer is empty and note how the placeholder looks.
- [ ] `components.placeholderMissingBase` (behavior, M6, handbook): A component that names a glyph absent from the font is drawn…
  - Observe: Add a component, delete its base glyph, and note the placeholder and whether a click selects it.
- [ ] `components.placeholderCircularReference` (behavior, M6, handbook): A component whose base glyph, directly or through nesting, contains the glyph…
  - Guess: Anchor propagation reports a glyph on or above a component cycle as an error, where glyphsLib gives glyphs on the cycle their own anchors and glyphs above it none; a cycle in the reference app settles what it shows.
  - Observe: Make glyph A contain B and B contain A (through a file edit if the app refuses) and note the placeholder.
- [ ] `components.nodesInsideComponentsSnap` (behavior, M6, handbook): While a node, anchor or other element is being dragged, the nodes…
  - Guess: The nodes inside components show for as long as any drag of a pointer tool lasts, and not while the pointer only hovers; dragging a node next to a component in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Drag a node next to a component and note when the component's nodes appear and which of them the node snaps to.
- [ ] `components.topBottomAnchorsPropagate` (behavior, M6, handbook): Anchors named top and bottom on a component's base glyph count as…
  - Guess: The editor and the compiler propagate anchors exactly as fontc does, every name included, since its algorithm gives top and bottom as the handbook says and also the other names both reference compilers export; a composite that inherits an ogonek or a cedilla anchor in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A non-master layer whose base glyph has no layer with the same id reads the base's master layer of the same master, where glyphsLib interpolates the base's anchors at the layer's axis location; a brace layer in the reference app whose base lacks it settles it.
  - Guess: A base glyph without a layer for the composite's master passes no anchors on, where glyphsLib stops with an error; the reference app with such a font settles it.
  - Guess: A component pinned to another master (`masterId`) passes on the anchors of that master's layer of its base, the layer alignment places it from; a composite with a pinned component in the reference app, read through a mark attached to it, settles it.
  - Guess: A smart component whose pole layers' outlines cannot be interpolated still has its anchors interpolated from the anchors those layers propagate, where glyphsLib stops with an error; the reference app with such a smart glyph settles it.
- [ ] `components.underscoreAnchorSnapsToPlainAnchor` (behavior, M6, handbook): A component whose base glyph has an anchor with a leading underscore…
  - Guess: A component whose underscore anchors find no plain anchor among the components before it is set after them as another base, as if typed; a mark added to a base without the matching anchor in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.alternativeAnchorSuffix` (behavior, M6, handbook): A layer may carry several anchors of one type, told apart by…
  - Guess: The component's anchor attribute is matched by its plain name, the part before the first underscore or period, against the mark's underscore anchor; the target is the attribute's anchor, else for a numbered name (top_2) the plain anchor of that base, else the plain anchor; a ligature with a mark set to top_1 after its second base in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.autoAlignDefaultOnWhenComponentsOnly` (behavior, M6, handbook): Automatic alignment is on by default for a glyph layer whose shapes…
  - Guess: A component that is not aligned keeps its stored position but still counts: its anchors serve the marks after it and, as a base, it moves the next base to follow its stored place; a composite with one disabled base among aligned ones in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.fontWideDisableAutoAlign` (behavior, M6, handbook): The Font Info › Other option Disable Automatic Alignment turns off automatic…
  - Guess: The font-wide option also overrides components whose alignment is forced (1); a forced component in a font with the option on, in the reference app, settles it.
- [ ] `components.autoAlignColors` (behavior, M6, handbook): Component fill colour signals alignment state in Edit View: green for auto-aligned,…
  - Guess: Components are shown by their outline drawn in the state colour, not filled; a composite in the reference app's Edit View settles it.
  - Guess: Every component aligned horizontally only is blue, the single Number component and a component set to horizontal alignment alike; a component set to horizontal alignment in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Open a glyph built from components only, then add a path to it, and note the colour of the components before and after.
  - Observe: Build `one.sups` or another glyph from a single figure component and note its colour.
- [ ] `components.singleNumberComponentShiftsVertically` (behavior, M6, handbook): When a composite's only component has the glyph category Number (zero to…
  - Guess: A Number-category single component with alignment 0 behaves as alignment 3, and alignment 3 gives the same horizontal-only alignment to any component; the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks the stored positions of both.
- [ ] `components.autoAlignMetricsFromBase` (behavior, M6, handbook): The sidebearings of an auto-aligned composite are derived from its base components,…
  - Guess: A layer with a path keeps its stored width even when it holds forced components, which are still placed; a forced component next to a path in the reference app, with the base's width changed, settles it.
- [ ] `components.autoAlignMetricsOffsetKeys` (behavior, M6, handbook): An auto-aligned composite accepts exactly one kind of metrics key, a signed…
  - Guess: Other keys already stored on an aligned layer, from a file or from before the glyph was aligned, are kept but ignored: not evaluated, not listed as out of sync, not applied by Update Metrics; an aligned composite with a key such as =a opened in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The spacing shortcuts of the Text tool skip glyphs aligned on the edited layer and list them, since alignment would put their components back; pressing Ctrl+Left on an aligned composite in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.autoAlignSingleComponent` (behavior, M6, handbook): A composite may consist of one auto-aligned component and then mirrors the…
  - Guess: A single aligned component keeps its transform; the composite takes the base glyph's width times the length of the component's transformed x axis (so a scaled component gives a scaled width), and a horizontally flipped component is shifted so its outline keeps the base glyph's sidebearings mirrored (nine from six keeps six's width); the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks the flipped single components the sources hold.
- [ ] `components.autoAlignBaseThenMarks` (behavior, M6, handbook): In a base-plus-marks composite the base component comes first in the shape…
  - Guess: The first base sits at x = 0 and y = 0 and keeps its scale, slant and rotation; only its offset is computed; a base flipped vertically keeps its stored vertical offset, since y = 0 would hang it below the baseline; the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks it.
- [ ] `components.marksStackInOrder` (behavior, M6, handbook): Several marks on one base stack in the order they were added:…
  - Guess: A mark's underscore anchor attaches to the anchor of that name the components before it give, as propagation computes it after each component (the most recent component that offers the name wins); a composite with two stacked marks and a third in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `components.multipleBasesSetSideBySide` (behavior, M6, handbook): Several base components in one composite are laid out next to each…
  - Guess: Each base after the first is placed at the sum of the advance widths before it, with no kerning, and the composite's width is the sum of the bases' widths; right-to-left glyphs are laid out the same way, in shape order; the corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks it on the sources' ligatures.
- [ ] `components.marksBelongToPrecedingBase` (behavior, M6, handbook): With several bases, shape order assigns marks: each base component is followed…
  - Guess: A mark looks for its anchor only on the nearest base before it and the marks attached since, never on an earlier base; a ligature whose second base lacks the mark's anchor, in the reference app, settles it.
- [ ] `components.exitEntryAnchorsConnectBases` (behavior, M6, handbook): Base components can be chained with cursive-attachment anchors: when one component's glyph…
  - Guess: Only the #-prefixed anchors connect components; the plain exit and entry anchors serve cursive attachment alone; the composite's width is the connected bases' widths after the shift, ending at the last base's advance. The corpus alignment measure (M6 plan § 5) checks it where sources use it.
- [ ] `smart.widthHeightFollowBoundingBox` (behavior, M6, handbook): With View › Show Bounding Box on, dragging a selected smart component's…
  - Guess: A box drag sets the value whose extent matches the dragged box, read linearly between the extents at the property's two ends, unclamped and rounded like other box values; dragging a knob in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: With several objects selected, a smart component among them scales like any component; scaling a smart component together with a path in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Rotating a smart component from outside its box rotates it, since the handbook rules out only resizing; trying it in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Select a smart component with a Width property, drag the right knob of its bounding box and note the Width value and whether it stops at the range ends.
- [ ] `smart.handlesFromPropertyAnchors` (behavior, M6, handbook): An anchor whose name equals a property name exactly (case included), present…
  - Guess: Dragging a handle sets the value whose interpolated anchor position is nearest the pointer, found by projecting the pointer on the line between the anchor's positions at the property's two poles, with no clamp; dragging a handle in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Add an anchor named after a property to every layer of a smart glyph, select a component of it and drag the grey handle; note the value it sets beyond the range ends.
- [ ] `corner.glyphNaming` (behavior, M6, handbook): A glyph is a corner glyph when its name starts with _corner
  - Guess: A layer with a corner that cannot be fitted (its path deleted, a zero-length segment next to it) is drawn in the editor without any of its corners, as the export leaves it; a hint whose path was deleted in the reference app shows what it draws.
  - Guess: Corners are fitted on the layer's stored shapes, whose indices the hints name, before its smart components are drawn; fontc draws smart components first and then reads the indices against the shapes that result, which differs only in a layer that has both. A layer with a smart component before a path with a corner, built in the reference app, settles it.
- [ ] `corner.highlightAndSelection` (behavior, M6, handbook): In Edit View a corner component is tinted with a faint blue…
  - Guess: The tint is a disc around the corner's node rather than a fill of the corner's own outline; a corner component in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Attach a corner component and note the shape and extent of its blue tint.
- [ ] `corner.copyPasteOntoNodes` (behavior, M6, handbook): A selected corner component can be copied with Edit › Copy and,…
  - Guess: Pasting a corner, cap, segment or brush onto a selection of the wrong kind (a corner with a segment selected, a cap on nodes that are not neighbours) is refused with a message and nothing changes; the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: One copied corner goes onto every selected node; several copied corners go onto as many selected nodes, in order, and any other count is refused. Copying two corners and pasting them onto three nodes in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Copy two corners, select three nodes and paste: note which nodes get which corner, or whether the paste is refused.
- [ ] `corner.mirrorViaPalette` (behavior, M6, handbook): The Palette's mirror buttons flip selected corner components, equivalent to negating the…
  - Guess: Mirroring negates the scale only and keeps the corner's alignment mode; mirroring a corner aligned left in the reference app and reading its Info box settles it.
  - Observe: Select a corner aligned left on a diagonal stem, press the horizontal mirror button and read the Info box: note the scale fields and the alignment button.
- [ ] `corner.entryAnchor` (behavior, M6, handbook): The entry point, where the stem runs into the corner, defaults to…
  - Guess: The entry anchor slides the fitted corner along its own baseline until the anchor lies on the incoming stroke, and the corner is then fitted as without it; the reference app's output for `av_left_anchoroffpath` agrees, and a left anchor on a curved stem in the reference app settles the rest.
  - Guess: A corner path faces right when its second node lies right of its start node; it then takes `right` as its entry anchor and `left` as its exit anchor. A right-facing corner glyph with both anchors in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A mirrored corner (one negative scale) ignores its entry and exit anchors; a mirrored corner with a left anchor in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `corner.exitAnchor` (behavior, M6, handbook): The exit point, where the outline leaves the corner back into the…
  - Guess: The exit anchor stands in for the end node when the corner is rotated and when the outgoing segment is cut (where it is nearest to the anchor); the end node stays where it is drawn and the outgoing segment runs on from it. A cupped serif in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `corner.scaleInterpolatesWithIntermediateLayers` (behavior, M6, handbook): If the corner glyph has Intermediate layers tagged with a Width and…
  - Guess: The percentages are read from the intermediate layer's name (for example `Width 150 Height 100`, a missing one is 100); on each axis the layers that vary it with the other axis at 100 % are interpolated piecewise linearly with the master layer at 100 %, scales beyond them are extrapolated, negative scales mirror after interpolation, and layers that vary both axes are not used. A corner glyph saved by the reference app with such a layer settles it.
- [ ] `corner.extraNodesAcceptCorners` (behavior, M6, handbook): Extra nodes, the points where overlapping paths intersect (displayed with View ›…
  - Guess: The two crossing segments are stored with the earlier path first (then the earlier node); a source saved by the reference app with a corner on an extra node settles which segment comes first.
- [ ] `pixel.requiresGridSpacingTwoOrMore` (behavior, M6, handbook): The Pixel tool works only when the font's Grid Spacing (Font Info…
  - Guess: With a grid spacing below 2 a click of the Pixel tool shows a message naming Font Info › Other › Grid Spacing and changes nothing; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `pixel.usesGlyphNamedPixel` (behavior, M6, handbook): Every pixel the tool paints is a component referencing the glyph named…
  - Guess: Pixel components are written with alignment off (-1), since a layer of components only would otherwise be aligned automatically; a pixel glyph saved by the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The pixel glyph exports like any glyph whose name has no underscore, and pixel components export as components (TrueType) or outlines (CFF) by the usual rules; a pixel font exported by the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.createComposite` (M6, handbook, live): Create Composite
  - Guess: The components are the full Unicode canonical decomposition of the glyph's code point, each code point mapped to its glyph name through the glyph database (U+0301 to acutecomb), suffixes carried over and the preferred-mark rules applied, plus our own table for glyphs Unicode does not decompose (i, j and ligatures by their name parts); the composites stored in corpus files saved by the reference app measure it (M6 plan § 5).
  - Guess: A glyph the database cannot decompose, or one whose parts are missing from the font, is refused with a message naming the missing parts and nothing changes; running it on such a glyph in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The hints that pointed at nodes of the replaced shapes (stems, corner and cap components) are removed with them, and hints without node references stay; running it on a glyph with corner components in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Create Composite keeps the layer's anchors and the glyph's metrics keys; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.addComponent` (M6, handbook, live): Add Component
  - Guess: The component is appended at offset zero, after every other shape, and the layer's automatic alignment places it; a component that would make the glyph contain itself is refused; adding a component in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.componentFromSelection` (M6, handbook, live): Component from Selection
  - Guess: The suggested name is `_part.` plus the current glyph's name, with `.001`, `.002` appended while the name is taken; the dialog of the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A name that is already a glyph is refused with a message; the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The new glyph keeps the shapes at their coordinates and the component sits at offset zero; anchors stay on the original layer; the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: On each other master the shapes with the same indices move into the new glyph's layer of that master, and a master whose layer lacks them gets an empty layer; a two-master font in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Without a name the command takes the suggested one; each layer of the new glyph takes the width of the original glyph's layer of its master; the reference app's new glyph settles it.
  - Guess: Only master layers are split: a non-master layer is refused, and images in the selection are refused; the reference app with a brace layer or an image selected settles it.
  - Guess: Hints move with the paths they point at, and a hint that links a moved and a kept path is removed; a hinted glyph in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `component.move` (M6, handbook, live): Move Component
  - Guess: A move of an aligned or locked component is refused with a message rather than silently left without effect (the font stays the same either way); a component aligned horizontally only takes the vertical part, and a purely horizontal move of it is refused; dragging such components in the reference app settles whether it shows a message.
- [ ] `component.setAnchor` (M6, handbook, live): Choose Attachment Anchor
  - Guess: The anchor menu lists the anchors of the attachment target whose name is the mark anchor's name without its underscore or that name followed by an underscore and a suffix; when the stored name no longer exists, the component attaches to the plain name and keeps the stored value; the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The command accepts only names the component can attach with: one of its own underscore anchors without the underscore (`top` for `_top`), or that name followed by an underscore and a suffix (`top_viet`, `top_2`); an empty name clears the choice; the anchor menu of the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.showGlyphsUsingAsComponent` (M6, handbook): Show all glyphs that use this glyph as a component
  - Guess: The glyphs open in a new Edit View tab, one per line slot in glyph order; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `component.enableAutomaticAlignment` (M6, handbook, live): Enable Automatic Alignment
  - Guess: Enable stores 0 (decided by the layer) on a layer of components only and 1 (forced) on a layer that has paths, so the item always aligns the component; enabling alignment on a component next to a path in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `component.lock` (M6, handbook, live): Lock Component
  - Guess: Lock Component is offered only for components that are not aligned automatically, and the command refuses an aligned component with a message; the context menu of an aligned component in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `glyph.decomposeGlyph` (M6, handbook, live): Decompose Glyph
  - Guess: The anchors that decomposition brings in are the propagated anchors the layer lacks, so mark attachment is unchanged; decomposing a composite with propagated anchors in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Decomposed paths take the component's place in the shape order and the layer's hints are renumbered to keep their nodes; a hinted composite in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A base glyph whose corner components cannot be fitted is decomposed without them, as the editor draws it, with a warning; the reference app with a broken corner settles it.
- [ ] `component.decompose` (M6, handbook, live): Decompose
  - Guess: Decomposing one level adds no anchors to the layer; a smart component becomes its interpolated outline; corner, cap, segment and brush components inside the base glyph are applied before its paths are copied. Decomposing such components in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: A smart component that cannot be interpolated is decomposed from its base layer, as the editor draws it, with a warning; the reference app with incompatible pole layers settles it.
- [ ] `smartGlyph.addProperty` (M6, handbook, live): Add Smart Property
  - Guess: A name another property of the glyph already has is refused; adding a second property with the same name in the reference app's Properties tab settles it.
- [ ] `smartGlyph.setProperty` (M6, handbook, live): Edit Smart Property
  - Guess: A rename carries the layers' poles and the values stored on every component of the glyph to the new name, and anchors named after the property keep their names; renaming a property in the reference app's settings window and saving settles it.
- [ ] `smartGlyph.removeProperty` (M6, inferred, live): Remove Smart Property
  - Guess: Removing a property drops its pole on every layer and leaves values stored on components in place and ignored; a minus button in the Properties tab of the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `smartComponent.setValue` (M6, handbook, live): Set Smart Component Value
  - Guess: A new smart component stores no values and draws with the poles of its master layer, as glyphsLib does with a missing value; a property removed from the glyph leaves the stored value in place and ignored; adding a smart component in the reference app and saving settles it.
  - Guess: A locked component refuses value changes; changing a value on a locked smart component in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `cornerComponent.add` (M6, handbook, live): Add Corner Component
  - Guess: A node that already holds a corner gets the new corner in its place, unscaled and aligned left, rather than a second corner; adding a corner to a node with a corner in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: An off-curve node and the first or last node of an open path are refused, because the fitting needs a segment on each side; the context menu on an open path's end node in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Select the end node of an open path and open the context menu: note whether Add Corner Component is shown, and what the corner looks like if it is added.
  - Observe: Add a corner to a node that already has one: note whether the old corner is replaced or a second one is added.
- [ ] `cornerComponent.decompose` (M6, handbook, live): Decompose Corner
  - Guess: A corner on an extra node, or one whose corner glyph does not exist, is refused instead of being removed without a trace; the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `cornerComponent.setScale` (M6, handbook, live): Set Corner Scale
  - Guess: A scale of 0 percent is refused with a message, because it collapses the corner to a point; typing 0 in the reference app's scale field settles it.

### Filters (36)

- [ ] `filter.target.visibleLayerOnly` (behavior, M3, handbook): A filter normally changes only the layer currently shown for each target…
  - Guess: A filter changes the visible layer of each targeted glyph unless `layers` is given; the handbook says only "normally".
- [ ] `filter.target.headlessExplicitTargets` (behavior, M3, inferred): Every filter command accepts explicit glyph names and layer ids so that…
  - Guess: Filters change paths only, not components, and a run over many glyphs is one undo step (11-filters open questions).
- [ ] `filter.hatchOutline.origin` (behavior, M3, handbook): Origin (X, Y) is the point the hatch pattern is anchored to;…
  - Guess: One hatch line passes through the origin (originX, originY).
- [ ] `filter.hatchOutline.stepWidth` (behavior, M3, handbook): Step Width is the spacing between neighbouring hatch strokes
  - Guess: stepWidth is measured at right angles to the lines, from 1 to 1000000 units.
- [ ] `filter.hatchOutline.angleConvention` (behavior, M3, handbook): Hatch angle: 0 degrees gives horizontal strokes, 90 degrees vertical strokes
  - Guess: The angle is in degrees counter-clockwise from horizontal, from -360 to 360.
- [ ] `filter.hatchOutline.openPathsWhenOffsetOff` (behavior, M3, handbook): Offset Path sets the stroke thickness
  - Guess: Each piece of line inside the outline is an open path of two line nodes, drawn in the direction of the angle.
- [ ] `filter.offsetCurve.signConvention` (behavior, M3, handbook): Positive Horizontal/Vertical values grow the outline, negative values shrink it; the two…
  - Guess: Outward is away from the filled side, read from nesting as Correct Path Direction does, so a path drawn the wrong way grows the same way.
- [ ] `filter.offsetCurve.makeStroke` (behavior, M3, handbook): Make Stroke offsets to both sides of the path and joins the…
  - Guess: Each side of a stroke reaches the full distances at position 50, so the stroke is twice the distance wide.
- [ ] `filter.offsetCurve.autoStrokeKeepsVerticals` (behavior, M3, handbook): Auto Stroke expands to both sides but keeps the shape's vertical dimensions…
  - Guess: Auto Stroke first squeezes the paths vertically about the middle of their bounds by the vertical distance at top and bottom, so the stroke ends at the original height; the method is ours.
- [ ] `filter.offsetCurve.positionDistribution` (behavior, M3, handbook): Position splits the stroke across the path: 0 % puts all of…
  - Guess: The left side reaches 2 · (1 − position) times the distances and the right side 2 · position times them.
- [ ] `filter.offsetCurve.keepCompatible` (behavior, M3, handbook): Keep Compatible forbids adding nodes or handles, so the result keeps the…
  - Guess: Keep Compatible moves each node to where the offset lines of its two segments meet and refits each pair of handles along the offset's directions, removing no overlap; the method is ours.
- [ ] `filter.offsetCurve.capStyles` (behavior, M3, handbook): Cap Style applies to the ends of open paths when making a…
  - Guess: The cap shapes are ours: flat cuts across the end, square extends by half the width, round is a half ellipse, round inset shortens the stroke by half its width before a round cap, and aligned cuts along the vertical or horizontal through the end node.
- [ ] `filter.roughen.segmentsThenJitter` (behavior, M3, handbook): Roughen works in two steps: curves and lines are first cut into…
  - Guess: Each segment is cut into equal pieces by length along the curve and the original nodes move too, so curves become polylines of line nodes.
- [ ] `filter.roughen.maxOffsetPerAxis` (behavior, M3, handbook): Horizontal and Vertical are upper bounds of the random displacement on each…
  - Guess: Each move is drawn uniformly from the box of plus or minus the bounds, turned by the angle, so the bounds are reached but never exceeded (11-filters open questions).
- [ ] `filter.roundCorners.selectionRule` (behavior, M3, handbook): Selected corners are rounded whatever their direction, so inward corners can be…
  - Guess: Selected corners are selected on-curve nodes and both end nodes of selected segments (11-filters open questions).
  - Guess: An open path has no filled side, so with no selection all its corners are rounded.
- [ ] `filter.roundCorners.visualCorrections` (behavior, M3, handbook): Visual corrections varies the effective radius by corner angle: sharp (acute) corners…
  - Guess: Visual corrections multiply the radius by tan(a / 2) for a corner of inner angle a, limited to between a quarter and four times the radius; the formula is ours.
- [ ] `filter.roundedFont.usesVerticalStems` (behavior, M3, handbook): Rounded Font reads the vertical stem metrics of the current master (Font…
