  - Guess: The kern lookups are split by script and direction as fontc splits them; the GPOS of a font with Latin and Cyrillic kerning exported from the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `autoFeature.dist` (behavior, M5, handbook): dist (Distances)
  - Guess: The scripts whose kerning goes to dist are fontc's (Indic, Universal Shaping Engine, Khmer, Myanmar); a font with Devanagari kerning exported from the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `Default Layer Width` (parameter, M5, handbook): Width in units for new glyphs and layers, replacing the built-in defaults…
  - Guess: Entries are looked up in the master's parameters before the font's, as for the other per-script parameters; the reference app with the parameter on a master would settle it.
  - Guess: A value that is not a number from 0 to 65535 is ignored; the reference app with such a value would settle it.
  - Guess: The CJK scripts are han, hangul, kana and bopomofo; a new layer of a glyph of each script in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `Link Metrics With First Master` (parameter, M5, handbook): Set on a master to mirror the spacing and kerning of the…
  - Guess: Spacing and kerning commands refuse to edit a linked master and name the master it follows; no source says what the reference does with such an edit.
- [ ] `Preview Descender` (parameter, M5, handbook): Master parameter, float, the counterpart of Preview Ascender for the lower edge
  - Guess: The default reads winDescent, not the winAscent the handbook names, because that text reads as a misprint; the reference app with a font whose winAscent and winDescent differ settles it.
  - Guess: A value of either sign is a distance below the baseline; the reference app with a positive and a negative value settles it.
- [ ] `components.importedFilesDisableAutoAlignSetting` (behavior, M6, handbook): A user setting, Disable Automatic Alignment for Imported Files, decides whether fonts…
  - Guess: The setting works through the font's own Disable Automatic Alignment option, which stays on and is saved with the font, rather than by setting each component's alignment to disabled; the Font Info of a UFO opened with the setting on, in the reference app, settles it.
- [ ] `Decompose Components` (parameter, M6, handbook): Decomposes all components at export, for environments that mishandle composites; file size…
  - Guess: A decomposed glyph keeps its own contours first, then its components' contours in the order the compiler decomposes a glyph that mixes contours and components (fontc's order); a composite exported by the reference app with the parameter on would settle it.
- [ ] `Decompose Glyphs` (parameter, M6, handbook): List of glyph names whose components are turned into outlines at export
  - Guess: A text value is read as glyph names separated by commas or spaces, besides the documented list; the reference app with a text value would settle it.

### Files: open, import, export (40)

- [ ] `file.open` (M1, handbook, live): Open…
  - Guess: The menu label and place are our assumption, and the shortcut is the platform convention; the chapter states neither.
- [ ] `roundtrip.lossReport` (behavior, M1, inferred): Every save or export to a format that cannot hold part of…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Every save or export to a format that cannot hold part of the document returns a structured list of losses (object path, concept, what was done: dropped, decomposed, moved to lib). The UI shows it as a warning before the write is committed and the command line prints it; with `strict` set the write fails instead.
- [ ] `glyphs3` (format, M1, docs): Glyphs file, format version 3
  - Guess: A gray colour `(gray, alpha)` is read as an RGBA colour with equal components, and the shape remembers which of its colours the file wrote as gray so that they are written as gray again. Settled by watching whether the reference app writes a colour picked from the gray swatches as gray.
  - Guess: A version 3 `strokePos` stored as text that is not a plain number stays a stored value, because it cannot be converted to the version 4 convention. Settled by finding a file whose stroke position is an expression.
- [ ] `io.stableSourceSerialization` (behavior, M1, memory): Saving writes source files in a normalised form, with fixed key order,…
  - Open (recalled, not confirmed): Saving writes source files in a normalised form, with fixed key order, number formatting and indentation, and rewrites only the glyph files that changed, so version-control differences show real edits only. RoboFont bundles a UFO normaliser; the exact save behaviour is recalled rather than read.
- [ ] `export.fileNaming` (behavior, M2, memory): Each exported file is named by the font's PostScript name (family and…
  - Guess: The PostScript-name stem is recalled from the reference, not read in a source; dropping characters a file name cannot hold and falling back to the source file's name are ours.
- [ ] `export.flavourSubfolders` (behavior, M2, inferred): An export that writes both outline flavours at once (file.exportAll, font.compile) puts…
  - Guess: An export of both flavours at once puts them into ttf and otf subfolders; how the reference lays out such an export is not documented.
- [ ] `file.exportAll` (M2, handbook, live): Export All
  - Guess: The reference's settings for Export All are not documented; using each document's last-used export settings is our assumption, and until FontCraft remembers settings the command takes them as parameters.
- [ ] `compile.removeOverlapsStatic` (behavior, M2, docs): fontmake removes overlaps by default when building static fonts and skips it…
  - Guess: A composite glyph keeps its components at export, so components that overlap each other keep their overlaps, following fontmake; how the reference app exports such a glyph is not documented.
- [ ] `import.outlinesSingleInEditView` (behavior, M3, handbook): In Edit View the same command takes a single file and places…
  - Guess: A single file imported in Edit View is appended to the active layer; the handbook does not say whether existing shapes are kept or which layer receives them.
- [ ] `paste.strokeAndFillMayDouble` (behavior, M3, inferred): The handbook advises Sketch users to copy shapes with a 1 pt…
  - Guess: A pasted shape with both fill and stroke may arrive as two contours; this is our reading of the handbook's advice, which gives no cause.
- [ ] `file.importOutlines` (M3, handbook, live): Outlines…
  - Guess: An SVG reads as one font unit per SVG unit with SVG y = 0 at the ascender; the handbook does not say how a drawing is scaled.
- [ ] `dialog.importOutlinesMissingGlyph` (panel, M3, handbook): Import Outlines: no matching glyph
  - Guess: One question covers every unmatched file, with the buttons Add Glyphs and Skip; the handbook asks once per file, with Add Glyph and OK (skip).
- [ ] `dialog.pastedOutlineBounds` (panel, M3, handbook): Pasted outline is outside the glyph
  - Guess: The correction only translates: it moves the pasted box's bottom left to the origin.
  - Guess: The buttons read Move and Keep Position, with the line "The pasted outline lies outside the glyph box." above the fields.
- [ ] `clipboard` (format, M3, inferred): Clipboard flavours for shapes, glyphs and kerning
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Copy places three flavours on the clipboard: our own JSON fragment of the model (shapes, anchors, whole layers or glyphs, or kerning pairs, with every attribute), an SVG path for drawing applications, and plain text. Paste prefers our JSON, then SVG, then PDF, as vector applications offer them; pasted artwork far outside the glyph box triggers the bounds-correction prompt and coordinates are rounded to the grid unless the grid is zero.
- [ ] `file.openRecent` (M4, inferred, live): Open Recent
  - Guess: The list holds the last ten documents opened or saved, newest first; a file that no longer exists is dropped when the list is shown. Settled by observing the Start Window.
  - Guess: File › Open Recent without an index or a path shows the Start Window's list of recent documents, since the menu bar has no submenu of files. Settled when the menu bar can list them.
  - Guess: A path is kept once, as an absolute path; opening or saving it again moves it to the top. Settled by observing the Start Window.
- [ ] `start.shownAtLaunchWithoutDocuments` (behavior, M4, handbook): The Start Window appears at launch when no document is open
  - Guess: With Show window on startup off, the app opens a new document at launch, as File › New does. Settled by observing a launch with the checkbox cleared.
- [ ] `file.newFromGlyphSets` (M4, handbook, live): New from Glyph Sets…
  - Guess: Phonetics and TransLatin sets are listed under latin, Coptic and the other Greek sets under greek. Settled by observing the reference's chooser.
  - Guess: A script picked without naming sets takes its Core set (Latin, Cyrillic, Greek and Arabic Core); a script without a default takes its first set. Settled by observing what the reference's chooser preselects when a script is switched on.
  - Guess: An encoded glyph takes the glyph database's name for its code point, an unencoded one the database's nice name for its source name; the source name is kept when the database knows neither. Settled by comparing a new document from the reference's chooser with the database names.
  - Guess: The new document keeps New's drawn .notdef first and adds no other glyph than the sets'; glyphs follow the default sort order. Settled by observing a new document from the reference's chooser.
- [ ] `glyphData.glyphSets` (M4, inferred, live): List Glyph Sets
  - Guess: A script id that stands for several Unicode scripts (`kana`) or none (`math`) reads as the id with a capital (`Kana`, `Math`). Settled by observing the reference's script names in its chooser.
- [ ] `window.start` (panel, M4, handbook): Start Window
  - Guess: Each glyph set's checkbox shows its glyph count after its name. Settled by observing the chooser.
  - Guess: Without earlier picks, Latin is included with its default sets. Settled by observing the chooser on a fresh install.
  - Guess: A script switched on takes its default sets (its Core set) checked; the checkboxes of a script that is not included are greyed out. Settled by observing the chooser.
  - Guess: While there are recent documents the chooser has a Recent Documents button that goes back to the list. Settled by observing the window.
  - Guess: The window closes when a document is opened or created. Settled by observing the window.
- [ ] `start.showAtLaunch` (setting, M4, handbook): Show window on startup
  - Guess: On by default, since the Start Window is described as appearing at launch. Settled by observing a fresh install.
- [ ] `export.productionNameLigature` (behavior, M4, docs): For an underscore-joined name each part is resolved to its production name,…
  - Guess: A ligature part whose production name has a dot is joined as fontc joins it (bh_a-telugu → uni0C2D.half0C05), not replaced by the cleaned glyph name as glyphsLib does. Settled by exporting such a ligature from the reference app.
- [ ] `ufo.openTypeCategories` (behavior, M4, docs): A UFO written from a font read from another format gets public.openTypeCategories…
  - Guess: glyphsLib classifies after propagating anchors into composites; a layer without anchors of its own counts its components' attaching anchors, which gives the same class for the usual accented composites. Settled by comparing with glyphsLib's UFOs of the corpus.
  - Guess: Custom glyph data files do not change the classes, as glyphsLib reads only its built-in data here. Settled by a reference export with a custom GlyphData file.
- [ ] `compile.productionNames` (behavior, M4, docs): By default glyphs are renamed to production names in the binary
  - Guess: Use Custom Naming (disablesNiceNames) keeps the source names at export, as the handbook's Don't use Production Names entry says it has the same effect; the reference compilers ignore it. Settled by exporting a font with Use Custom Naming on from the reference app.
- [ ] `compile.productionNameSanitising` (behavior, M4, docs): Final names are stripped of every character outside A-Z, a-z, 0-9, period…
  - Guess: A name that is empty after cleaning, or longer than the 255 bytes post can store, becomes glyphNNNNN (by glyph id) with a warning; fontc would write the empty or long name. Settled only by a reference that writes such a font.
- [ ] `export.afmIsLossy` (behavior, M5, handbook): AFM export is for exchange with other editors and drops what the…
  - Guess: Group pairs are expanded to glyph pairs with the exceptions applied, so the AFM file holds the kerning that applies to every covered glyph pair; the handbook does not say.
  - Guess: AFM values stay in the font's units and are not scaled to 1,000 units per em, so a round trip loses nothing; Adobe's note assumes 1,000. An AFM exported by the reference from a 2,048-unit font would settle it.
- [ ] `open.kerningMostlyPreserved` (behavior, M5, handbook): Pair kerning and kerning groups (classes) from a compiled font are mostly…
  - Guess: A class read from a compiled font becomes a kerning group named after its first glyph in glyph order; no source says how the reference names recovered groups.
  - Guess: A glyph keeps the first class it is in on each side, and pairs of a later class with other members are stored as glyph pairs; opening a font whose subtables use overlapping classes in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: A `kern` feature record used only by right-to-left scripts fills the right-to-left kerning, any other the left-to-right kerning; opening an Arabic font in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: A pair two feature records of one direction cover (for two scripts) takes the first record's value; no source says what the reference does.
- [ ] `import.metricsModes` (behavior, M5, handbook): Glyph metrics from a metrics or AFM file are applied in one…
  - Guess: The default mode is LSB and width (the RSB follows); the handbook gives no default.
- [ ] `file.exportMetrics` (M5, handbook, live): Export Metrics
  - Guess: The `metrics` format is our kerning-text format (`kerning-text` in 30-formats.toml), because the reference does not document its own metrics file; files in the reference format are neither read nor written.
  - Guess: A metrics file holds the pairs of one direction (left to right by default, `direction` for the others), because kerning-text has one direction line; an export of a master with right-to-left or vertical pairs needs one file per direction. A reference metrics file with such pairs would settle it.
  - Guess: The metrics file's extension is .tsv; the reference's own extension is not documented.
- [ ] `file.importMetrics` (M5, handbook, live): Metrics…
  - Guess: The import goes to the master given, else the first master, and `sourceMaster` defaults to the first master of the source file.
  - Guess: Importing kerning replaces the master's pairs in each imported direction, so export then import gives back the same table; the handbook does not say whether it merges. Importing into a master with pairs in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: Import Kerning and Import Kerning Classes default to on (Import Kerning off for a Glyphs file, which cannot give kerning values), and Import Metrics to off; the handbook states no defaults.
  - Guess: A metrics file also sets the left, right and width keys as it gives them, an empty key field removing the key the field shows; an AFM leaves the keys as they are.
  - Guess: Width, Outline Centred rounds the new LSB half up, so the outline stays on whole units; the handbook does not say.
  - Guess: Selected Glyphs limits glyph metrics and kerning classes; kerning values are imported whole.
  - Guess: Selected Glyphs means the glyphs selected in Font View, also while an Edit View tab shows; the handbook does not say which selection. Ticking it with a tab open in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `dialog.exportMetrics` (panel, M5, handbook): Export › Metrics
  - Guess: The format, file name and folder sit on the tab, with the folder chooser for the folder, because the dialog framework has no save-file chooser; the handbook puts them in a save dialog after Next…. A save-file chooser in the shell would settle it.
  - Guess: The tab shows a Master pop-up that starts on the toolbar's master, so a master can be exported without changing the toolbar; the handbook names no such control.
- [ ] `dialog.importMetricsFile` (panel, M5, handbook): Import Metrics (Metrics or AFM file)
  - Guess: The two dialogs are two tabs of one window opened from the menu, with the file typed as a path, because the dialog framework has no file chooser field; the handbook asks for the file first and then shows the dialog that fits it. A file field with a chooser would settle it.
- [ ] `dialog.importMetricsFont` (panel, M5, handbook): Import Metrics (Glyphs or UFO file)
  - Guess: The tab can import from another open document as well as from a file; the handbook names files only.
  - Guess: The tab offers Selected Glyphs and a Source Master field; the handbook does not say whether this dialog has them.
- [ ] `afm` (format, M5, docs): Adobe Font Metrics
  - Guess: A glyph's code is its first Unicode value when that is at most 255, else -1; Adobe's note means a code in the font's encoding. An AFM exported by the reference would settle it.
- [ ] `compile.kernFeatureWriter` (behavior, M5, docs): Generated kerning is split into lookups by script and writing direction using…
  - Guess: Where the featureWriters setting asks for what fontc refuses to build (ignoreMarks off, a quantization other than 1, kernFeatureWriter2), FontCraft writes the kerning with the standard settings and warns; a build with fontmake would settle what the setting does there.
  - Guess: The featureWriters setting is read from the font's user data, then from the built master's; fontc reads only a .glyphs font's user data, while glyphsLib also carries a master's into its UFO lib.
- [ ] `open.alignsCompositeMetrics` (behavior, M6, handbook): On opening a font file, composite glyphs are put under automatic alignment…
  - Guess: A UFO counts as an imported file and is aligned on opening, because the Source Formats page points a UFO to the importing behaviours and gives it its own Disable Automatic Alignment option in Font Info; the running app opening a UFO with an out-of-date composite would settle it.
  - Guess: Aligning on opening is not an undo step and does not mark the document as edited; the reference app's edited mark after opening a UFO with an out-of-date composite settles it.
- [ ] `import.metricsBreaksAutoAlignment` (behavior, M6, handbook): If an imported value for an automatically aligned composite differs from what…
  - Guess: Imported values that round to the derived ones (less than half a unit away) leave alignment on; importing a metrics file whose values differ by fractions in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: Alignment is switched off only on the layer of the master being imported, for every component of that layer; the other masters' layers stay aligned; importing into one master of a two-master font in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `compile.glyphsOutlinePreSteps` (behavior, M6, docs): Two Glyphs-specific steps run before decomposition when the source asks for them:…
  - Guess: A glyph whose anchor propagation fails keeps its own anchors and the build goes on with a warning; exporting a source with a component cycle from the reference app settles what it does.
- [ ] `compile.markFeatureWriter` (behavior, M6, docs): Anchors generate mark (mark to base and mark to ligature), mkmk (mark…
  - Guess: A top-level include in the feature code keeps all five generated features out until M8, as it keeps the generated kerning out, since the included file may hold any of them; compiling the included file (M8) settles it.
  - Guess: Anchors whose names the reference compilers refuse (`_`, `_top_1`, a number 0), whose ligature number is above 1,024, or whose positions do not fit 16 bits are left out with a warning, where fontc stops the build; an export of such anchors from the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `compile.gdefClasses` (behavior, M6, docs): GDEF glyph classes come from an explicit GDEF block in feature code…
  - Guess: A static master's classes are worked out from that master's anchors only, as fontmake does for each master's UFO, where fontc looks at the anchors of every master; a source whose masters differ in anchor names would settle it.
- [ ] `compile.gdefLigatureCarets` (behavior, M6, docs): Ligature caret positions in GDEF come from anchors named caret_N (horizontal text,…
  - Guess: Carets at the same position are all kept, as fontc keeps them, where ufo2ft keeps one; a ligature with two carets at one position in the reference app's export would settle it.

### Settings and the window (8)

- [ ] `edit.undo` (M0, memory, live): Undo
  - Guess: The menu label and Cmd+Z are recalled from the reference, not read in a source.
- [ ] `edit.redo` (M0, memory, live): Redo
  - Guess: The menu label and Cmd+Shift+Z are recalled from the reference, not read in a source.
- [ ] `file.close` (M0, memory, live): Close
  - Guess: The Save, Don't Save and Cancel question and Cmd+W are recalled from the reference, not read in a source.
- [ ] `dialog.livePreview` (behavior, M3, inferred): A dialog is a form over its command's parameters
  - Guess: Every filter dialog previews its result live and OK keeps it as one undo step; a tutorial video shows this for Transformations only.
  - Guess: Dialogs remember their last values for the session, and their first values are ours (11-filters open questions).
- [ ] `settings.get` (M4, inferred, live): Get Setting
  - Guess: Our own command; the reference has no headless access to settings, so there is nothing to settle.
- [ ] `settings.set` (M4, inferred, live): Set Setting
  - Guess: Our own command; the reference has no headless access to settings, so there is nothing to settle.
- [ ] `appearance.displayUnicodeValue` (setting, M4, handbook): Display Unicode Value
  - Guess: On shows the code point, off the small marker, as the handbook's wording implies. Settled by observation.
  - Guess: Off by default (the small marker), as the handbook gives no default. Settled by observing a fresh install.
- [ ] `sampleStrings.groups` (setting, M5, handbook): Sample Strings
  - Guess: The built-in default holds groups of our own choosing: spacing strings built around n, o, H and O with placeholders, figures, and traditional pangrams for Latin, Greek and Cyrillic; no source lists the reference defaults we may copy.

### Elsewhere (4)

- [ ] `audit.run` (M3, docs, live): Run Audit
  - Guess: A source's instances are compiled as TrueType for the binary checks whatever outline format the source exports, and at most 32 of them; a run of the reference auditors on a source would settle whether the CFF flavour or every instance should be read.
  - Guess: On a source, the family checks receive every instance compiled from it, the variable font included; checks that should compare only the static instances would need the reference tools' behaviour on sources to settle it.
- [ ] `audit.fix` (M3, docs, live): Fix Issues
  - Guess: An outline fix on a composite glyph only reports unless `safeOnly` is false (21-qa question 4).
- [ ] `audit.toggleLiveAudit` (M3, inferred, live): Live Audit
  - Guess: Live audit is off when the app starts; the default is ours.
- [ ] `audit.toggleMarkers` (M3, inferred, live): Show Audit Markers
  - Guess: Markers are on when the app starts; the default is ours.

## Later milestones (after M6) (676)

### Edit View: drawing, guides, layers and the Palette (39)

- [ ] `anchors.ccmpAlongsideMark` (behavior, M8, handbook): When the mark feature is generated and glyphs such as idotless and…
  - Guess: ccmp follows from the glyph names alone, whether or not the marks carry anchors for the mark feature; Update in the reference app on a font with idotless and an anchorless acutecomb would settle it.
- [ ] `image.supportedFormats` (behavior, M11, handbook): The reference accepts every image type its operating system can decode and…
  - Guess: PDF and PSD images are kept as references and drawn as an unsupported-image marker with a message, not decoded. Settled by the owner's answer to M11 § 7 question 3.
  - Observe: Image font: add a PDF and a PSD image to a glyph; note how each is drawn.
- [ ] `image.storedAsRelativePath` (behavior, M11, handbook): The document records only the image's path relative to the font file
  - Guess: A file outside the document's folder is stored with a path that climbs out of it (`../scans/A.png`), as the handbook says only a relative path is stored. Settled by adding an image from another folder in the reference and reading the saved path.
  - Guess: An image added to a document that has never been saved keeps its absolute path until the first save, which rewrites it relative to the new file. Settled by adding an image to an untitled document in the reference and saving it.
  - Guess: A stored path that names a network share or a device (`\\host\share`, `//host/share`, `\\?\`, `\\.\`) is never read, on every platform, and the image counts as unreadable; absolute paths and paths climbing out with `..` are read. Settled by opening, in the reference, a file whose image path names a network share.
  - Observe: Image font: save, move H.png away and reopen the file; note how the missing image is drawn and whether a message appears.
  - Observe: Image font: add an image from a folder beside the document's folder, save, and read the stored imagePath.
- [ ] `image.defaultPlacement` (behavior, M11, handbook): A newly placed image sits at the layer origin, scaled so that…
  - Guess: The image's size in points comes from the resolution stored in the file (PNG pHYs, JPEG JFIF or Exif, TIFF resolution tags); a file without one counts as 72 pixels per inch, one pixel per unit. Settled by adding a 144 dpi PNG in the reference.
  - Observe: Image font: choose Glyph › Add Image with H.png, then with a 144 dpi copy; note the position and scale of each, and the pos and scale values stored in the file.
- [ ] `glyph.addImage` (M11, handbook, live): Add Image…
  - Guess: A stored image path that starts with two separators (`//host/share`, `\\host\share`) is refused on every platform, not only on Windows: a document comes from anywhere, and on other systems such a path is either a network share or one whose meaning the system decides. Settled by opening, in the reference app on a Mac, a file whose image path starts with `//`.
  - Guess: Two files that name the same glyph (A.png and A.jpg) refuse the call instead of one winning. Settled by Add Image with both files in the reference.
  - Guess: A layer holds one placed image, as the version 3 format stores one: Add Image on a layer that has an image replaces it. Image shapes of the version 4 format are read, drawn and written back but not created. Settled by adding a second image to a glyph in the reference and reading the saved file.
  - Observe: Image font: add a second image to H; note whether it replaces the first or both stay, and how the file stores them.
  - Observe: Image font: in Font View, Add Image with H.png and H.jpg together; note which one H receives and any message.
- [ ] `image.setTransform` (M11, handbook, live): Set Image Transform
  - Guess: Width and height are the size of the whole image, so a crop does not change them. Settled by cropping an image in the reference and reading the Info box.
  - Guess: Width and height are the displayed size in font units; rotation turns the image about its position point (its bottom-left corner before rotation), as the stored `pos`, `scale` and `angle` suggest. Settled by rotating an image in the reference's Info box and reading the saved file.
  - Observe: Image font: type a rotation in the image Info box and save; note the pivot of the rotation and the stored pos, scale and angle values.
  - Observe: Image font: crop H's image, then read the width and height in the Info box; note whether they are the whole image's or the cropped part's.
- [ ] `image.setCrop` (M11, handbook, live): Crop Image
  - Guess: The crop rectangle is in image pixels, measured from the image's bottom-left corner, as origin and size. Settled by cropping an image in the reference and reading `crop` in the saved file.
  - Observe: Image font: Option-Control-drag a handle of H's image to crop it and save; note the coordinate space of the stored crop rectangle (image pixels or font units, and from which corner).
- [ ] `image.setCropToLayerBounds` (M11, handbook, live): Set Crop to Layer Bounds
  - Guess: The layer bounds are the rectangle from 0 to the advance width and from the master's descender to its ascender. Settled by Set Crop to Layer Bounds on an image larger than the glyph in the reference.
  - Observe: Image font: choose Set Crop to Layer Bounds on H's image; note the rectangle used (advance width by the vertical metrics, or the outline bounds).
- [ ] `palette.pluginSections` (behavior, M13, handbook): Plug-ins can register their own Palette sections, which appear alongside the built-in…
  - Observe: Install a palette plug-in from the Plugin Manager, relaunch and note where its section appears among the built-in Palette sections and whether it collapses like them.
- [ ] `path.selectTunniLines` (M13, docs, live): Edit Tunni Lines
  - Guess: A segment counts as inside the selection when one of its handles is selected, both its end nodes are, or the segment itself is, as for Fit Curve; FontLab does not define it, and selecting nodes in FontLab with Tunni lines on would settle it.
- [ ] `path.setCurveTension` (M13, docs, live): Set Curve Tension
  - Guess: Where a segment's handles are parallel or diverge, so they have no intersection, tension is shown as a dash and Set Curve Tension leaves the segment unchanged with a warning; FontLab does not say (open question 9 in docs/spec/90-secondary-references.md).
  - Guess: A zero-length handle has no direction, so its segment counts as having no Tunni point and is left unchanged too; running the command on such a segment in FontLab would settle it.
  - Guess: With no segments named the command acts on every curve segment of the layer, and it also takes a list of glyphs; FontLab documents only the selection.
  - Guess: An absolute tension must be 1 to 100 percent and a relative step keeps each handle within 1 to 100; FontLab gives no range, and entering larger values in its Node panel would settle it.
  - Guess: A relative step is added to each handle's own tension, so unequal handles stay unequal; FontLab does not say whether it balances them first.
- [ ] `path.harmonizeNodes` (M13, docs, live): Harmonize Nodes
  - Guess: At an inflection (the far handles on opposite sides of the tangent) the two curvatures are made equal in size, since equal signed curvature is impossible there; running Harmonize on an S-curve in FontLab would settle it.
  - Guess: A smooth node with a straight side, or a selected node that is not between two curve segments, is left unchanged and listed in the result; FontLab does not say.
- [ ] `path.harmonizeHandles` (M13, docs, live): Harmonize Handles
  - Guess: G2 keeps the sum of the two handle lengths at the node and only divides it differently; FontLab does not say which lengths it keeps, and comparing handle lengths before and after Harmonize Handles in FontLab would settle it.
  - Guess: G3 changes the lengths of the two neighbouring handles along their own directions, by the smallest change found, so the neighbouring nodes keep their tangents; FontLab does not describe Superharmonize's method.
  - Guess: With no node selected both orders act on every smooth node of the layer, as Harmonize Nodes does; FontLab documents only the selection.
- [ ] `path.makeInkTrap` (M13, docs, live): Make Ink Trap
  - Guess: The two copies move half the width each, perpendicular to the corner's bisector, with the copy ending the incoming segment on that segment's side, and their neighbouring handles move with them; the reference only says the nodes move apart 'intelligently', so watching it trap an inside and an outside corner would settle it.
  - Guess: Without an inkTrapWidth parameter the width is 2% of the em; the reference's default for a new font would settle it.
  - Observe: Make Ink Trap on the inside corner of an L with ink trap width 20: record where the two nodes land.
- [ ] `path.loopCorner` (M13, docs, live): Loop Corner
  - Guess: Each segment goes on past the corner by `size`, which defaults to half the master's first vertical stem (4% of the em without stems); the reference does not say how far its loops reach, so measuring one would settle it.
  - Observe: Loop Corner on a rectangle corner in a master with a 100-unit vertical stem: measure how far each segment goes past the corner.
- [ ] `path.replaceWithCorner` (M13, docs, live): Replace with Corner
  - Guess: One segment or gap per path per call, and neighbours whose extensions meet behind them are refused rather than shortened; trying both in the reference would settle it.
- [ ] `path.alignToMask` (M13, docs, live): Align Points to Background
  - Guess: Points snap to background nodes and handles alike, and a moved on-curve node takes its unselected handles along; aligning a curve's node to a background node in the reference would settle it.
- [ ] `path.collapsePoints` (M13, docs, live): Collapse Points
  - Guess: The centre is the middle of the selected nodes' bounding box, rounded to the grid, and at least two on-curve nodes are needed; collapsing three uneven nodes in the reference would settle it.
- [ ] `path.scaleToSameSize` (M13, docs, live): Same Width / Same Height
  - Guess: A glyph counts as uppercase when its case says so or one of its code points is an uppercase letter; trying a glyph without code points in the reference would settle it.
- [ ] `edit.storeSelection` (M13, docs, live): Store Selection
  - Guess: Selected anchors and components are stored with the nodes, by anchor name and shape index; FontLab documents node selections only, and its panel on a selection with a component would settle it.
  - Guess: Storing an empty selection is refused; FontLab's Add button with nothing selected would settle it.
- [ ] `edit.getStoredSelections` (M13, inferred, live): Get Stored Selections
  - Guess: A query that maps stored selections onto any layer is our addition for agents and scripts; FontLab has no such command.
- [ ] `edit.recallSelection` (M13, docs, live): Recall Selection
  - Guess: On a layer where only part of a stored selection resolves (an incompatible master), the parts that resolve are selected and the reply counts the rest as missing; recalling where nothing resolves is refused. FontLab's panel on an incompatible master would settle it.
- [ ] `guide.makePowerGuide` (M13, docs): Make Power Guide
  - Guess: Moving a power guide is one undo step for every glyph it reshapes, and locked glyphs keep their outline while the rest move, with a warning naming them (open question 3).
- [ ] `path.findOutlinePattern` (M13, docs): Find Outline…
  - Guess: Matching is point for point only, as FontForge documents; shapes that are equal with another point structure do not match (open question 12).
- [ ] `path.findIntersections` (M13, docs, live): Add Nodes at Intersections
  - Guess: Crossings of a path with itself count too, and touching curves get a node like crossing ones; the manual speaks only of overlapping contours.
  - Guess: Locked paths get no nodes but still cross the others; the manual does not mention locks.
- [ ] `path.makeParallel` (M13, docs, live): Make Parallel
  - Guess: With two lines, the moved line keeps its length and turns about its fixed end to the nearer of the two parallel directions; the manual only says the lines become parallel.
- [ ] `path.spacePoints` (M13, docs, live): Space Points Evenly
  - Guess: The lowest and highest points stay and the others keep their order; the manual only says the points are spaced regularly.
  - Guess: Regions join points whose coordinates along the spacing axis are at most `within` apart (default 100 units), and the centres of the regions' extents are spaced evenly; the manual gives no distance and no reference point.
  - Guess: An on-curve node's handles move with it unless they are selected too.
- [ ] `path.roundToCluster` (M13, docs, live): Round to Cluster
  - Guess: x and y are clustered on their own, every node and handle included; a cluster grows while values are at most `within` (default 1 unit) above its lowest, and takes the value most of its members have (ties: nearest the mean, then the lower). The manual gives no distance and no rule for the shared value.
- [ ] `path.makeArc` (M13, docs, live): Make Arc
  - Guess: Of the many ellipses through the two nodes with the path's directions there, FontCraft takes the quarter ellipse framed by the two tangent lines (a quarter circle when they meet square at equal distances), and half an ellipse when the directions are opposite; the manual does not say which ellipse it picks beyond preferring one with a node on an axis.
  - Guess: At an open end without a neighbouring segment the segment's own direction is used, and the result is always a cubic segment.
- [ ] `path.insertNodeAt` (M13, docs, live): Insert Node at Coordinate…
  - Guess: A node goes in at every place the segment crosses the coordinate, and a segment that does not reach it is refused; the manual does not cover several or no crossings.
- [ ] `path.nameContour` (M13, docs, live): Name Contour…
  - Guess: An empty name removes the name; the manual does not say how a name is cleared.
- [ ] `font.compareLayers` (M13, docs, live): Compare Layers…
  - Guess: Two layers match when their advance widths, their paths (closed or open, node count, each node's kind and position) and their components (glyph and placement) agree in order; anchors, hints and images are not compared. Watching the secondary reference compare layers with different anchors would settle it.
  - Guess: The tolerance applies on each axis to node positions, advance widths and component offsets, and defaults to 0; component scale, rotation and slant must match exactly. The secondary reference's dialog would settle it.
- [ ] `edit.copyLayerToLayer` (M13, docs, live): Copy Layer to Layer…
  - Guess: The mode defaults to replace, and replace keeps the target's images; widths, anchors and the background are not copied. The secondary reference's dialog defaults would settle it.
  - Guess: A glyph without one of the two layers is skipped and listed rather than refusing the command. The secondary reference has font-wide layers, so nothing there settles it.
- [ ] `view.setReferenceFont` (M13, docs, live): Set Reference Font
  - Guess: The language code picks a glyph named like the found one plus .locl and the code in capitals (A.loclTRK for TRK); applying the reference font's own locl substitutions through the shaper would settle what the reference app does.
  - Guess: Adding a path selects that font and turns the display on; the reference app's panel would settle whether adding also shows it.
  - Guess: Any font the open path reads (sources too, not only compiled fonts) can be a reference, drawn from its first master.
  - Observe: In Fontra, drop a compiled font on the Reference font panel, type a character in the custom character field and a language code such as 'tr', and note which glyph is drawn behind the edited one and in what colour.
- [ ] `palette.selections` (panel, M13, docs): Selections
  - Guess: The section sits after Transformations in the Palette and shows only the edited glyph's entries; FontLab's panel placement would settle it.
  - Guess: Add with a name that already exists replaces that entry rather than refusing; observing the panel's Add on a duplicate name would settle it.
  - Observe: In FontLab 8, store a selection named 'stem' in one master, switch to another master and click the entry: check whether the same node indexes are selected and whether a mismatched master selects part or nothing.
- [ ] `palette.referenceFont` (panel, M13, docs): Reference Font
  - Guess: Fonts are added by typing or pasting a path; dropping files onto the section is not built. The reference app's drop behaviour would settle whether anything else happens on drop.
  - Guess: The list keeps at most 16 fonts and loads only the selected one; the reference app does not document a limit.
- [ ] `curveTension` (parameter, M13, docs): Default curve tension (percent) used by tension-driven drawing tools such as the…
  - Guess: A master without the parameter, or with a value outside 1 to 100, uses 55 percent, close to a circle's tension; FontLab's default value is not documented and a new FontLab font would show it.
- [ ] `thickness` (tool, M13, docs): Thickness
  - Guess: A drag with no modifier changes the width on the dragged side only, at the dragged node or at every picked control point; the release notes list only the modified drags, and watching the tool would settle it.
  - Guess: Shift+drag changes the path's stroke width by twice the drag distance and adds the drag distance to both sides of every node with its own widths, so the modulation is kept; the release notes do not say what happens to nodes with their own widths.
  - Guess: Shift+Alt+drag acts on the whole path: it changes the stroke position and moves the per-node widths of that path sideways by the same amount, each side kept at 0 or more.
  - Guess: Between two nodes the left and right widths change linearly with the curve parameter; the release notes do not describe the interpolation.
  - Guess: Once any node has its own widths the pen is round (the stroke height is not used), and round-inset and axis-aligned caps end the stroke as butt caps.
  - Guess: Delete clears the picked control points' per-node widths as Backspace does.
- [ ] `path.setPenPoint` (fidelity, inferred): Set Pen Point
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Writes or clears the pen-point data of a node or of a free pen point, the action behind the pen-point fields of the Info box. Clearing the data is how a pen point on a node is removed. The reference exposes this only as Info box fields.

### Edit View: text mode and preview (14)

- [ ] `textToken.syntax` (behavior, M7, handbook): In text fields that support them, `{{{token}}}` is replaced by a computed…
  - Guess: A token FontCraft cannot resolve stays in the text as written, and the compile report names it, as glyphsLib leaves it; a misspelt token in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: In font A (m7-one), type `{{{familyName}}} {{{nonsense}}}` into the description field, export and read name ID 10: note what the unknown token becomes.
- [ ] `textToken.versionString` (behavior, M7, handbook): `{{{versionString}}}` gives the application's version number
  - Guess: The token gives FontCraft's full release number (`0.0.1`), where the handbook's example shows two parts (`3.4`); the reference app's output for a release number of three parts would settle the form.
- [ ] `textToken.keyPath` (behavior, M7, handbook): In a font or instance context the token may be a property…
  - Guess: The supported key paths are the font's familyName, versionMajor, versionMinor, unitsPerEm (and upm), date (as the source writes it), copyright, designer, designerURL, manufacturer, manufacturerURL, license, licenseURL, trademark, description, sampleText, vendorID, uniqueID, and the instance's name, familyName, fullName and postscriptFullName (family and style name with a space), postscriptFontName and fontName (family without spaces, a hyphen, the style name), preferredFamilyName, preferredSubfamilyName, weightClass (400 when unset) and widthClass (5 when unset); tokens tried in the reference app settle more.
  - Guess: In an instance context a key the instance does not have (designer, copyright…) reads the font's value, where glyphsLib keeps such a token as written; the same token typed into an instance's field in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: In font A (m7-one), type `{{{familyName}}} {{{fullName}}} {{{designer}}} {{{nonsense}}}` into the description field, export and read name ID 10: note which tokens expand.
- [ ] `textToken.numberValue` (behavior, M7, handbook): In a master context the token may be the name of a…
  - Guess: A master that stores no value for a defined number value reads 0, as a missing metric value does; the reference app with a number value added after the master settles it.
- [ ] `textToken.glyphProperty` (behavior, M7, handbook): `{{{glyph:path}}}` reads a layer property of a glyph, as in feature-code glyph…
  - Guess: In a font or instance context the token reads the first master's layer (an instance does not interpolate the value yet); the reference app with `{{{H:width}}}` in an instance's name settles it.
  - Guess: LSB and RSB of an empty layer name nothing and stay as written; the reference app with `{{{space:LSB}}}` settles it.
  - Guess: Numbers are written without a fraction when whole and with at most three decimals otherwise; a glyph with a fractional width in the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: The bounds are spelled as the reference's scripting object names them (`bounds.origin.x`, `bounds.size.width`); the reference app with those tokens settles it.
- [ ] `font.expandTextTokens` (M7, inferred, live): Expand Text Tokens
  - Guess: Tokens that name nothing are left as written and listed; a name field with an unknown token exported from the running app would settle it.
- [ ] `text.shaping` (behavior, M8, handbook): Edit View text is shaped with the compiled feature code: the substitution…
  - Guess: Only the features active in the Features menu apply, with no default features, so Arabic text shows its positional forms only when init, medi and fina are on; typing Arabic in an Edit View tab of the reference app with no feature active would settle it.
  - Guess: A glyph typed by name is shaped as a private-use character that maps to that glyph, so substitutions apply to it as to typed characters; typing /a.sc next to a feature that substitutes it in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: Kerning is applied by the tab's kerning mode to the glyphs after substitution, and the compiled kern feature is not used in Edit View; a font whose hand-written kern feature has a contextual rule would settle it.
  - Observe: In F2 (Arabic), type Arabic text in an Edit View tab with no feature active: note whether positional forms show.
  - Observe: In F1 (Latin features), type `/a /a.sc /f /i` in Edit View and turn on smcp and liga: note whether the glyphs typed by name are substituted.
  - Observe: In F1 (Latin features), write a contextual rule in a hand-written kern feature, compile and view the pair in Edit View: note whether the contextual value shows.
- [ ] `textPreview.shaping` (behavior, M8, handbook): Text Preview shapes its text as an application would: every feature that…
  - Guess: The preview's font is rebuilt from the current feature code, kerning and anchors after edits, once editing pauses for half a second, without pressing Compile; editing a feature without compiling while the reference app's Text Preview is open would settle it.
  - Observe: In F1 (Latin features), edit a feature without pressing Compile while Text Preview shows text the feature affects: note whether the preview follows the edit, and when.
- [ ] `textPreview.unicodeBidi` (behavior, M8, handbook): Text Preview orders mixed right-to-left and left-to-right text by the Unicode Bidirectional…
  - Observe: In F2 (Arabic), type "12 34" inside right-to-left text in Text Preview: note the order of the digits.
- [ ] `editView.setLocalizedForms` (M8, handbook): Localized Forms
  - Guess: The menu offers the script and language pairs that the font's enabled locl entries name in their `script` and `language` statements, in order of appearance; the menu's entries in the reference app would settle it.
  - Observe: In F1 (Latin features), turn on Localized Forms in Edit View, choose Turkish and type i: write down the menu's script and language entries and note the glyph shown.
- [ ] `editView.getFeatures` (M8, inferred): Get Preview Features
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Query returning the features available in the Features menu, which are active in the tab, and the chosen script and language.
- [ ] `preview.respectsInstanceData` (behavior, M9, handbook): Instance previews take intermediate and alternate layers into account and apply some,…
  - Guess: The preview of an instance applies the instance's interpolation parameters (Local Interpolation, InterpolationWeightY, manual interpolation), Rename Glyphs and Remove Glyphs, and no filters; settle by previewing an instance with a filter parameter in the reference app.
  - Observe: In F1 (two masters), put a filter parameter on the Regular instance and preview Regular: note whether the preview applies the filter.
- [ ] `preview.render` (M9, inferred): Render Preview
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Renders the preview line to an image so an agent can inspect what the Preview Area would show. Not a reference feature.
- [ ] `proofing.webfontPreview` (behavior, M12, handbook): Checking .woff2 (and optionally .woff for old browsers) in the export dialog…
  - Observe: Any font: export WOFF2 alone, then WOFF2 with WOFF; note whether the app writes an HTML test page itself or only the font files.

### Components and anchors (33)

- [ ] `anchor.setContext` (M8, inferred): Set Context
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Stores the context code of a contextual anchor, the action behind the Anchor Context field. The reference exposes it only as that field.
  - Observe: In F6 (contextual anchors), type a context with a token into a `*top` anchor's Anchor Context field and save: note the user data key that stores it.
- [ ] `palette.anchorContext` (panel, M8, handbook): Anchor Context
  - Observe: In F6 (contextual anchors), select a `*top` anchor in Edit View: note the Anchor Context field in the Palette and how it accepts a context with a token.
- [ ] `cap.attachesToTwoAdjacentNodes` (behavior, M13, handbook): A cap component is bound to two nodes that are neighbours on…
  - Observe: Select two nodes that are not neighbours on one path, then two nodes on different paths, and open the context menu: note whether Add Cap Component is shown, disabled or missing.
- [ ] `cap.node1Node2AnchorSetup` (behavior, M13, handbook): Flexible anchor setup for a cap glyph: two anchors named node1 and…
  - Guess: With node1 and node2 anchors the cap is mapped by the similarity transform that sends node1 onto the first host node and node2 onto the second (uniform scale, rotation, translation); settled by exporting `_cap.round` on the 80- and 120-unit stems.
  - Observe: Add `_cap.round` (anchors node1 and node2 50 units apart) to the 80-unit stem of `l` and the 120-unit stem of `i`, export and send back: compare the cap's height in both to see whether scaling is uniform or along the stem end only.
- [ ] `cap.highlightAndSelection` (behavior, M13, handbook): A cap component is tinted with a faint blue background in Edit…
  - Observe: Screenshot a selected and an unselected cap at 400% zoom to record the highlight colour and how the selection is drawn.
- [ ] `cap.copyPasteOntoSegments` (behavior, M13, handbook): A selected cap component can be copied and then pasted onto other…
  - Observe: Copy a selected cap, select two adjacent nodes on another stem and paste; then select a single node and paste: note what is pasted in each case.
- [ ] `segment.startEndAnchors` (behavior, M13, handbook): The start and end anchors belong on the first and last node…
  - Observe: Move the end anchor of `_segment.wave` 40 units left of the end node and look at an attached segment component: note how much shorter it is.
- [ ] `brush.glyphNaming` (behavior, M13, handbook): A glyph is a brush glyph when its name starts with _brush
  - Observe: Give `_brush.arrow` a second closed path and add the brush: note whether it is refused, uses the first path only, or merges both.
- [ ] `brush.startEndAnchors` (behavior, M13, handbook): Glyph › Set Anchors (Cmd+U) places a brush glyph's start and end…
  - Guess: Set Anchors puts a brush glyph's start at (0, 0) and its end at (advance width, 0); Set Anchors on a brush glyph in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `capComponent.add` (M13, handbook): Add Cap Component
  - Guess: The cap's open path replaces the host segment between the two nodes and is joined to the path at both ends, as a corner's open path replaces part of the host outline; settled by exporting a cap test font from the reference app and comparing the outline.
  - Observe: In the cap test font (a `_cap.flag` glyph with an `origin` anchor and an open path, an `l` with an 80-unit stem), select the two top nodes of the stem, choose Add Cap Component from the context menu, pick `_cap.flag`, then export an OTF and send it back: its `l` outline shows where the cap's path goes and whether the stem's top segment is replaced.
- [ ] `capComponent.decompose` (M13, handbook): Decompose Cap
  - Guess: Decompose Cap writes the fitted outline into the host path in place of the cap and keeps the node order of the host before the cap; settled by decomposing a cap in the reference app and comparing node order with the exported outline.
  - Observe: After adding `_cap.flag` to `l`, right-click the cap, choose Decompose Cap and save the file: compare the stem path's nodes with those of the exported OTF from the Add Cap Component check.
- [ ] `capComponent.setFit` (M13, handbook): Fit Cap to Stroke
  - Guess: With Fit on, the cap is scaled along the stroke-end direction by the ratio of the host's node distance to the cap's reference width (the distance between node1 and node2, or the cap's advance width with only an origin anchor); its other direction is not scaled. Settled by the Fit export check.
  - Observe: Add `_cap.flag` (origin anchor only) to the 80-unit stem of `l` and to the 120-unit stem of `i`, export with Fit off and again with Fit on: compare the cap outlines in the four results for scale along and across the stem end.
- [ ] `capComponent.setGlyph` (M13, handbook): Replace Cap Component
  - Observe: Click the name of an attached cap in its Info box and note what opens (the cap picker, a text field or a menu) and whether the new cap keeps the old one's scale and alignment.
- [ ] `capComponent.setScale` (M13, handbook): Set Cap Scale
  - Guess: The cap Info box has the corner Info box's horizontal and vertical scale fields, applied after fitting and before placement; settled by the Info box check.
  - Observe: Select an attached cap with View › Show Info on and list every control of its Info box, with default values; type 50 into each scale field you find and export, to see whether scale applies before or after Fit.
- [ ] `capComponent.setAlignment` (M13, handbook): Set Cap Alignment
  - Guess: For a cap, left aligns to the incoming segment of the first node, right to the outgoing segment of the second, both to their mean direction and none keeps the cap unrotated; settled by exporting the slanted-stem cap with each mode.
  - Observe: Add `_cap.flag` to the slanted stem of `slash` and export once per alignment mode the Info box offers: note the cap's rotation in each export.
- [ ] `segmentComponent.add` (M13, handbook): Add Segment Component
  - Guess: On a straight host segment the segment glyph's path is scaled along the baseline so start lands on the first node and end on the second, and is not scaled across; settled by the straight-segment export check.
  - Guess: On a curved host segment each point of the segment glyph, handles included, is moved to the host curve at the same fraction of its length and offset along the curve's normal by its distance from the baseline; settled by the curved-segment export check.
  - Observe: In the segment test font (`_segment.wave`: a three-node open path on the baseline with start and end anchors on its end nodes), add the segment component to the bottom straight segment of a square and to one curved segment of an `o`, export an OTF and send it back: the outlines show the length and normal mapping, and whether the node count stays three.
- [ ] `segmentComponent.decompose` (M13, handbook): Decompose Segment Component
  - Observe: Decompose a segment component on the `o` and save: compare the resulting path with the exported outline of the Add Segment Component check.
- [ ] `segmentComponent.setGlyph` (M13, handbook): Replace Segment Component
  - Observe: Click the name in a segment component's Info box: note what opens and whether the replacement keeps the attachment.
- [ ] `segmentComponent.editGlyph` (M13, handbook): Edit Segment Glyph
  - Observe: Click the arrow button in a segment component's Info box: note whether the segment glyph opens in the same tab after the current glyph or in a new tab.
- [ ] `brush.add` (M13, handbook): Add Brush
  - Guess: The brush outline between start and end follows the host segment as segments do; the parts before start and after end are moved rigidly with the host's direction at its two ends; settled by the brush export check.
  - Guess: With nodes selected, each selected node becomes the end node of a brush on the segment that ends there; a node at the start of an open path gets no brush. Settled by the node-selection check.
  - Observe: In the brush test font (`_brush.arrow`: one closed arrow outline, start anchor at the tail, end anchor where the head begins), add the brush to a straight open path and to a curved open path, export an OTF and send it back: the head should keep its shape and the shaft follow the curve.
  - Observe: Select only the last node of a three-segment open path and choose Add Brush: count how many brushes are added and on which segments.
- [ ] `brush.decompose` (M13, handbook): Decompose Brush
  - Observe: Decompose a brush on the curved open path and save: note whether the open host path is removed and replaced by the closed outline.
- [ ] `brush.setGlyph` (M13, handbook): Replace Brush
  - Observe: Click the name in a brush's Info box and note what opens.
- [ ] `brush.editGlyph` (M13, handbook): Edit Brush Glyph
  - Observe: Click the arrow button in a brush's Info box: note where the brush glyph opens.
- [ ] `infoBox.capComponent` (panel, M13, handbook): Cap component Info box
  - Observe: Screenshot the Info box of a selected cap, segment component and brush with View › Show Info on; list each control and its default.
- [ ] `dialog.capPicker` (panel, M13, handbook): Cap glyph picker
  - Observe: Open Add Cap Component on a font with three cap glyphs and two corner glyphs: confirm only cap glyphs are listed and note the sort order and what the search field matches.
- [ ] `recipe.directives` (behavior, M13, docs): Recipe text accepts directives beside glyph names: a comment marker for ignored…
  - Guess: A recipe has one line of components; directives may follow it on that line or stand on lines of their own, several `#!` lines are joined with line breaks, and the note is written when the recipe is set, not at every rebuild.
  - Guess: An unknown `#?` keyword is refused, and `noanchors` is matched without regard to case; the reference's handling is not documented.
- [ ] `anchor.positionExpressions` (behavior, M13, docs): An anchor's x and y may each be an expression instead of…
  - Guess: FontCraft's variable names are ascender, descender, xHeight, capHeight, baseline and width, plus `anchor:<name>.x`, `guide:<name>`, `zone:<name>` and `param:<name>` references; the reference's spellings (x_height, caps_height) are read as aliases. Nothing settles it in the primary reference, which has no anchor expressions.
  - Guess: An expression that names something missing, divides by zero or gives a value that is not finite leaves the anchor where it was and is reported in the command's warnings; the reference's handling is not documented.
  - Guess: When expressions are worked out again after other commands, failures leave the anchors in place without a warning; `anchor.setExpressions` reports them.
  - Guess: Results are rounded to the font's grid, as typed anchor positions are.
  - Guess: An anchor with expressions that is moved by hand goes back to its expressions' values at once; removing the expressions frees it.
  - Guess: Anchors are worked out once per change, in layer order, so `anchor:NAME` sees the new position of an anchor before it and the old position of one after it.
  - Guess: `guide:NAME` is the y of a horizontal guide and the x of a vertical one (`.x` and `.y` pick either), looked up on the layer and then on the master; `zone:NAME` is a master metric by name, then by kind; `param:NAME` is a master number value, then a numeric font custom parameter.
- [ ] `component.copyTransformation` (M13, docs, live): Copy Component Transformation
  - Guess: A target entry without `glyph` or `layer` means the source glyph and the source layer's id; the reference copies to whatever is selected, so it has no such default.
  - Guess: An automatically aligned target takes the scale, rotation and slant but keeps the position alignment gives it on the aligned axes; the reference has no automatic alignment, so nothing settles this but use.
- [ ] `font.detectComposites` (M13, docs, live): Detect Composites
  - Guess: A match needs every point of the other glyph's outline within 0.5 unit after the best translation, or after a translation and flip when `flips` is on; FontLab gives no tolerance.
  - Guess: Only glyphs whose layer has closed paths and no components serve as bases; running it in the reference app on a font whose base glyphs are themselves composites would settle it.
  - Guess: Bases are tried from the most nodes down, a base must have fewer nodes than the target or as many and come earlier in the glyph order (no cycles), and among bases of equal size one whose name (without suffix and 'comb') appears in the target's name goes first; comparing the reference app's choice between identical outlines (period and dotaccentcomb) would settle it.
  - Guess: Without `layer` or `layers` every master layer is searched, each on its own, and a glyph whose masters got different components is warned about; the reference app's behaviour on a multi-master font would settle it.
  - Guess: A mirrored match is a mirrored copy drawn in the opposite direction, as a mirrored component draws once decomposed; the offset is rounded to the font's grid when the match still holds.
  - Guess: A detected component keeps the default automatic alignment when aligning puts it where the outline was (within the tolerance) and leaves the width as it is; alignment is turned off on the components it would move, and on all new components of a layer whose width it would change, as the primary reference's Import Metrics does for metrics that alignment would change. Checking the alignment setting of components the reference app detected, with and without matching anchors, would settle it.
- [ ] `path.detectComponent` (M13, docs, live): Detect Component
  - Guess: The first glyph in font order whose same-id layer has only closed paths equal to the selection wins, within 0.5 unit after a translation (and a flip with `flips`); selecting an outline shared by two glyphs in the reference app would settle which one it picks.
  - Guess: No matching glyph is an error that changes nothing; the reference app's message or silence when nothing matches would settle it.
  - Guess: The new component keeps the default automatic alignment unless aligning would move it beyond the tolerance or change the width, and then alignment is off on it; alignment is judged on the layer as it ends up, so a component next to remaining paths keeps the default. Checking the alignment setting of a component the reference app detected would settle it.
  - Observe: Draw a copy of a glyph's outline in another glyph, select it and run Detect Component: note which glyph the component names and where it sits.
  - Observe: Select an outline no glyph has and run Detect Component: note the message, if any.
- [ ] `glyph.setAutoLayerRecipe` (M13, docs, live): Set Auto Layer Recipe
  - Guess: `masters` defaults to every master, which covers the reference's companion action that copies the recipe to all masters; seeing whether that action is a command of its own would settle it.
  - Guess: Without `recipe`, a glyph keeps the recipe one of its layers has, else takes the components its name decomposes into by Create Composite's rules; the reference's default recipe is not documented.
  - Guess: An automatic layer holds only the recipe's components: paths or components added by hand are replaced at the next rebuild; whether the reference lets one draw on an automatic layer would settle it.
  - Guess: A recipe whose components contain the glyph is refused, and a layer whose recipe becomes circular later stays empty.
  - Guess: Recipe parts are glyph names only, without placement suffixes such as `@anchor`.
- [ ] `anchor.removeExpressions` (M13, docs, live): Remove Anchor Expressions
  - Guess: Without `glyph` or `glyphs` it acts on every glyph, and locked glyphs are skipped and listed; the reference acts on the selection.
- [ ] `anchor.setExpressions` (M13, docs, live): Set Anchor Expressions
  - Guess: Expressions are set by a command of their own, where the reference types them into the anchor's coordinate fields; a syntax error refuses the command.

### Filters (5)

- [ ] `filter.adjustStems` (M13, docs, live): Adjust Stems
  - Guess: Both edges of a stem move by half the change, so the stem keeps its centre line; the secondary reference running a rule on a stem away from the sidebearings would settle it.
  - Guess: A horizontal stem's edge on the baseline, x-height, cap height, ascender or descender stays and the other edge takes the whole change; the secondary reference on a serif sitting on the baseline would settle it.
  - Guess: A round stem (the sides of o) is adjusted by moving the extreme nodes on both sides with their handles; one without a node at either measured point is left alone.
  - Guess: A rule that would make a stem narrower than 1 unit makes the whole command fail; the secondary reference with an offset larger than a stem would settle it.
  - Guess: One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on.
- [ ] `filter.changeWeight` (M13, docs, live): Change Weight…
  - Guess: The offset moves nodes and handles without adding nodes, so curves are only approximately parallel to the original; an outline comparison against the secondary reference would settle how close it must be.
  - Guess: Method auto picks cjk for CJK ideographs, Hangul and kana and lcg for everything else; the secondary reference's documentation on auto would settle it.
  - Guess: Default zones: bottom at the baseline, top at the x-height for lowercase glyphs (by code point, else by the first letter of the name) and the cap height for the rest, serif height 0; the secondary reference's dialog defaults would settle them.
  - Guess: Counters auto means retain with lcg and squish with cjk; retain moves each vertical stem right by the amount times its index so the counters keep their widths, and the advance width grows by the amount times (stems − 1).
  - Guess: Squish keeps the advance width, so the outline grows into both sidebearings by half the amount each.
  - Guess: Cleanup (Remove Overlap after the change) is off by default.
  - Guess: One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on.
- [ ] `filter.condenseExtend` (M13, docs, live): Condense / Extend…
  - Guess: The counter add applies only to counters between two stems; the parts of the outline left of the first stem and right of the last (arms, serifs) only scale. The secondary reference on a T would settle it.
  - Guess: A glyph with no vertical stem scales as one piece by the counter scale.
  - Guess: Counters scaled below zero width become zero width.
  - Guess: One work budget covers the whole command across all glyphs and layers; past it the command fails, names the glyph where it stopped and changes nothing. Our own limit; the secondary reference on a very large selection would show whether it stops or runs on.
- [ ] `filter.changeXHeight` (M13, docs, live): Change x-Height…
  - Guess: Above the x-height band the outline stretches so the master's ascender stays where it is, and nothing above the ascender moves; the secondary reference on b or h would settle whether ascenders move instead.
  - Guess: Below the serif height nothing moves (descenders included) and the band within the serif height of the x-height shifts rigidly.
  - Guess: The master's x-height metric is not changed; the command changes outlines only.
- [ ] `filter.nonLinearTransform` (M13, docs): Non-Linear Transform…
  - Guess: The expressions use anchor expressions' grammar (M13.16) with the variables x and y and the functions sin, cos, tan, sqrt, abs, min and max; segments are split so no segment covers more than 1/32 of the glyph's height before mapping. FontForge's grammar is not followed.

### Spacing and metrics keys (3)

- [ ] `spaceCenter.inputSyntax` (behavior, M13, docs): The spacing text accepts plain characters, /name for a glyph by name,…
  - Guess: Space Center takes the reference spellings /name, /name@layer, /?, /!, /:group and \n unchanged; Edit View's text mode keeps the primary reference's escapes only.
- [ ] `spacing.autoSpace` (M13, docs): Auto Space
  - Guess: Method optical is FontCraft's own (open decision D11, docs/plans/M13.md § 3): for each side, the white area between the glyph's side profile, clipped to a depth below its extreme, and the sidebearing line, measured over a band from the baseline to the reference height plus overshoot, is made equal to a target area; target, depth, band and sampling step are parameters, with defaults calibrated on the control glyphs n, o, H and O of the font itself. Neither FontLab nor FontForge documents its method.
- [ ] `view.showMetricsBeam` (M13, docs): Metrics Beam
  - Guess: The beam is one height per font, shared by Edit View and Space Center, and sidebearing commands take it as an optional `beam` parameter; FontLab's and RoboFont's pages do not say whether it is per font or per window.

### Kerning and the Kerning window (4)

- [ ] `kerning.groupClassNames` (behavior, M8, handbook): Every kerning group is available in feature code as a glyph class…
  - Guess: Only the `@MMK_L_`/`@MMK_R_` classes the code refers to and no class entry defines are written, before the first entry, with the glyphs the target exports in font order, and a group without such glyphs stays undefined so the compiler names the reference; the expanded code of a reference export settles it.
- [ ] `kerning.autoKern` (M13, docs): Auto Kern
  - Guess: The proposed value is the target separation minus the pair's optical gap, the mean horizontal distance between the left glyph's right profile and the right glyph's left profile over the same band and depth clipping as auto-spacing, rounded to the nearest 5 units by default; the target separation defaults to the optical gap of a reference pair (n n for lowercase, H H for capitals) without kerning. FontLab documents neither K1 nor K2 (open decision D11).
- [ ] `kerning.kernToDistance` (M13, docs): Kern to Distance
  - Guess: The gap is the smallest horizontal distance between the two outlines at any height, measured on the decomposed and overlap-removed outlines; FontLab says only that the pair's minimum horizontal gap becomes the value.
- [ ] `kerning.buildGroups` (M13, docs): Build Kerning Groups…
  - Guess: Two glyphs fall in one group while the root mean square of their side profiles' differences over the band stays under maxDifference; FontForge does not describe its grouping rule.

### Font View (13)

- [ ] `smartFilter.rule.custom` (behavior, M8, handbook): Rule Custom holds a free-form glyph predicate written in the same expression…
  - Guess: The language adds matches (a regular expression over the whole value), which fea-rs refuses, because the Glyph Name rule offers matches and compiles to this language; && and || stand for AND and OR, and a yes-or-no attribute alone means == true. A comparison on tags holds when it holds for one tag. Settled by trying these forms in the reference's Custom rule.
- [ ] `font.features.reorder` (M8, inferred, live): Reorder Entries
  - Guess: Entries are dragged within their group, and the order of features is the order of feature blocks and of chained suffixes; the running app would settle whether dragging exists.
  - Observe: In F1 (Latin features), drag a feature entry to another place in the sidebar: note whether it moves and whether the order is kept on save.
- [ ] `fontView.image.batchByFileName` (behavior, M11, handbook): When Add Image is given several files, each is matched to a…
  - Guess: Files with no glyph of their name are skipped and listed in the result; no glyph is created. Settled by Add Image with an unmatched file in the reference.
  - Observe: Image font: in Font View, Add Image with H.png, I.png, J.png and a.png; note which glyphs receive an image and any message about J, which has no glyph.
- [ ] `fontView.image.caseSensitivity` (behavior, M11, handbook): Glyph names are case-sensitive but many file systems are not, so A.png…
  - Guess: File names are matched to glyph names exactly, case included, on every platform. Settled by Add Image with `a.png` in a font with `A` and `a` in the reference.
  - Observe: Image font: in Font View, Add Image with H.png, I.png, J.png and a.png; note whether a.png goes to a only or also to A.
- [ ] `fontView.image.showImageOption` (behavior, M11, handbook): View → Show Image controls whether placed images are drawn in Font…
  - Observe: Image font: in Font View turn Show Image off; note whether H (which has paths) is drawn without its image and I (empty) with its image.
- [ ] `view.filterByEncodingFile` (M13, docs, live): Filter by Encoding File
  - Guess: Encoding files are read in three line forms (`name slot`, `0xSlot 0xCodePoint`, and one name or code point for the next slot) with `%%FONTLAB ENCODING:` and `%%FONTLAB CODEPAGE:` titles; FontLab's own encoding and code page files would settle the forms it accepts.
  - Guess: Without `path`, `name` picks a built-in code page (ASCII, Latin 1, Windows 1252, Mac OS Roman); with `path` it names the filter. FontLab's list of bundled encodings would settle which ones ship.
  - Guess: A code-point slot the font has no glyph for lists the glyph data's name for that code point, and running the command again with the same name replaces the list filter instead of adding a second one.
  - Observe: Open an encoding file with gaps in FontLab 8 and check whether the Font window draws a cell for each empty slot and for each slot whose glyph the font lacks.
- [ ] `font.compareInTable` (M13, docs, live): Compare Fonts in List
  - Guess: Each font is read in its first master, and without `fonts` every open document is compared; FontLab's Font window comparison mode would settle which master and fonts it uses.
  - Guess: Measured values are rounded to hundredths of a unit before rows are marked as differing; the reference's own comparison display would settle the precision.
  - Observe: In FontLab 8, turn on font comparison in the Font window with two fonts of a family and note the properties offered, which master each column reads and how differing values are marked.
- [ ] `glyph.listDependencies` (M13, docs, live): Show Dependencies
  - Guess: Glyphs that use the glyph through other composites are listed too, with their depth and the glyph they reach it through, nearest first; the reference's documentation does not say whether nested references are shown.
- [ ] `edit.pasteAfter` (M13, docs, live): Paste After
  - Guess: Only whole copied glyphs paste after; a drawing fragment copied from a layer has no advance width and is refused. Pasting a selection from an outline window in the secondary reference would settle it.
  - Guess: Paths and components are pasted; anchors are not, so a word glyph does not get several anchors of one name. The secondary reference's result with anchored glyphs would settle it.
  - Guess: Each copied glyph gives its layer of the target's master (by id, else by name, else the master shown when copying), as edit.paste matches masters.
- [ ] `font.renameGlyphsByNamelist` (M13, docs, live): Rename Glyphs by Name List…
  - Guess: An unencoded glyph named after a renamed glyph plus a suffix is renamed with it (uni0041.sc to A.sc); the reference's documentation does not say, and renaming such a font in it would settle it.
  - Guess: Renamed glyphs keep their code points and their places in the glyph order, and when a code point is named twice in a list the first name counts.
  - Guess: A glyph with several code points is renamed by its first code point only, and keeps its name when the list does not name that one; renaming such a glyph in the reference would settle it.
  - Guess: With updateReferences (the default) components, metrics keys, kerning, groups and the glyphOrder parameter follow the new names, as Rename Glyph does.
- [ ] `font.setStatusDefinitions` (M13, docs, live): Status Definitions…
  - Guess: The definitions are stored in the font's user data as a list of code, name, colour and default flag; Fontra's storage is not documented.
  - Guess: Two definitions with the same code or the same name, or more than one default, are refused; checking the reference's Status definitions panel would settle it.
  - Guess: Redefining the list keeps every glyph's stored code, even one the new list lacks (such glyphs show no tint); renaming or deleting a status in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `glyph.setStatus` (M13, docs, live): Set Status
  - Guess: FontCraft stores the status per glyph, in the glyph's user data, so `.glyphs` and UFO keep it; per-source statuses would move it to layer user data. The reference documentation does not say.
  - Guess: A glyph without a stored status is shown in the default status; setting a status on a new glyph in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: The tint fills the advance box from the descender to the ascender at a quarter of the status colour's opacity, and shows whenever the glyph is in a defined status (no display toggle yet); the reference's Development status color option would settle the look and the default.
- [ ] `palette.relatedGlyphs` (panel, M13, docs): Related Glyphs & Characters
  - Guess: A glyph's base name is the part of its name before the first dot, and alternates are the other glyphs with the same base name; the reference's documentation does not define the suffix rule.
  - Guess: The used-as-component section lists direct users only; Show Dependencies lists the nested ones.
  - Guess: The decomposition sections use Unicode's canonical decompositions, one level and full together, so U+1EA4 lists both U+00C2 and U+0041, and characters the font has no glyph for are listed dimmed.
