- [ ] `layer.setColorPaletteIndex` (M11, handbook, live): Set Palette Color of Layer
  - Guess: The headless command accepts any index up to 65,533, also one beyond the rows of the font's palettes, so layers can be set up before the palettes grow. Settled by opening a file whose colorPalette index is beyond its palettes in the reference.
- [ ] `layer.convertToIColor` (M11, handbook, live): iColor
  - Guess: Without `size` a new iColor layer gets 128 pixels per em. Settled by the label of a new iColor layer in the reference.
  - Observe: sbix font: add a new iColor layer; note the default size shown in its label.
- [ ] `layer.setIColorSize` (M11, handbook, live): Set iColor Size
  - Guess: The layer's placed image is rescaled about its bottom-left corner so it keeps unitsPerEm ÷ size units per pixel. Settled by the size change in the observe step.
  - Observe: sbix font: double-click an iColor layer entry and type 256; note the new label and whether the image is drawn half as large as at 128.
- [ ] `layer.setSvgImage` (M11, handbook, live): Set SVG Image
  - Guess: The SVG's viewBox is placed with its bottom-left corner at the layer origin, one SVG user unit per font unit, y pointing up. Settled by dropping an SVG file on an svg layer in the reference.
  - Observe: SVG font: drop F.svg on the svg layer of F; note where the viewBox lands and at which scale.
- [ ] `layer.convertToColor` (M11, handbook, live): Color
  - Observe: Palette font: open the context menu of a master layer in the Layers palette; note whether the colour layer kinds are offered for it.
- [ ] `attributes.addAttribute` (M11, handbook, live): Add Attribute
  - Guess: Shadow and Inner Shadow have blur, offset and colour fields, as the file format stores them; Pattern Image has an image reference. Settled by adding each in the reference's inspector.
  - Guess: A new Shadow or Inner Shadow is black at 50 % opacity with blur 10 and offset (10, −10) font units; Pattern Image keeps only a file reference, in a FontCraft key. Settled by adding each in the reference and reading the saved file.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: add Shadow, Inner Shadow and Pattern Image to a path; note the controls each one shows.
- [ ] `attributes.setStrokeWidth` (M11, handbook, live): Set Stroke Width
  - Guess: A stroke added to a path without one is opaque black. Settled by entering a stroke width in the reference's Attributes inspector.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: give a path a stroke width in the Attributes inspector; note the default stroke colour.
- [ ] `attributes.setStrokeAlignment` (M11, handbook, live): Set Stroke Alignment
  - Guess: The left button is inside and the right button outside, for paths in the usual direction. Settled by choosing each in the reference and reading `strokePos` in the saved file.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: on a counter-clockwise path with a stroke, choose left, then right stroke alignment; note which side the stroke moves to and the strokePos value saved in the file.
- [ ] `attributes.removeStroke` (M11, handbook, live): Remove Stroke
  - Guess: The removed stroke's colour is transparent black. Settled by reading `strokeColor` in a file saved after the minus button.
- [ ] `attributes.removeGradient` (M11, inferred, live): Remove Gradient
  - Guess: The Gradient row has a minus button, like the Stroke and Fill rows, that removes the gradient. Settled by looking at the Gradient row of the reference's Attributes inspector.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: on a path with a gradient, look for a way to remove the gradient (a minus button or another control); note what it is.
- [ ] `attributes.addGradient` (M11, handbook, live): Add Gradient
  - Guess: A new gradient is linear, from the path's fill colour at offset 0 to the same colour fully transparent at offset 1, running from the left middle to the right middle of the path's bounds. Settled by adding a gradient in the reference.
  - Guess: Conic gradients and the extend mode, which the version 4 file format has and the handbook does not describe, are accepted as parameters; the inspector shows only linear and radial. Settled by looking for them in the reference's inspector.
  - Guess: A new radial gradient is centred in the path's bounds and runs from radius 0 to half the bounds' width; a new conic gradient is centred and turns 360°. Settled by switching a new gradient to radial in the reference and reading the saved file.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: add a gradient to a path; note its type, its stop colours and its start and end positions.
- [ ] `attributes.setGradientType` (M11, handbook, live): Set Gradient Type
  - Guess: Switching the type places the gradient as a new gradient of that type and keeps its stops and extend mode. Settled by switching a moved gradient between linear and radial in the reference.
- [ ] `attributes.addGradientStop` (M11, handbook, live): Add Gradient Stop
  - Guess: A new stop takes the colour of the gradient at its offset. Settled by clicking the gradient bar in the reference.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: click the gradient bar between two stops; note the colour of the new stop.
- [ ] `attributes.setGradientStopColor` (M11, handbook, live): Set Gradient Stop Color
  - Guess: A stop can be moved along the bar, which `offset` does. Settled by dragging a stop knob sideways in the reference.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: drag a stop knob along the gradient bar; note whether the stop moves and how its offset changes.
- [ ] `attributes.setGradientPosition` (M11, handbook, live): Set Gradient Position
  - Guess: Without `end`, the end moves by as much as the start, so the gradient moves as a whole. Settled by dragging the start knob of a linear gradient in the reference's Edit View.
- [ ] `palette.layers.colorLayerEntry` (panel, M11, handbook): Color layer entries in the Layers palette
  - Observe: Palette font: add a layer to a glyph and choose each colour kind from its context menu in turn; note the entry's label, indent, bold text and swatch or size, and whether a later kind replaces the earlier one.
- [ ] `palette.layers.paletteColorChooser` (panel, M11, handbook): Palette color chooser
  - Guess: The chooser and the Edit View preview always use palette 0. Settled by opening the chooser in a font with two palettes in the reference.
  - Observe: Palette font (two palettes): click the swatch of a palette layer in the Layers palette; note which palette's colours the chooser lists.

### Files: open, import, export (46)

- [ ] `export.instanceExportFolder` (behavior, M7, handbook): An instance carrying the Export Folder custom parameter is written to that…
  - Guess: The sub-path is used as written, `..` segments included, as the handbook says; an absolute path, an empty segment, a folder name with a character such as `:` or `\`, or a path of more than 32 segments makes export skip that instance with the reason, one trailing `/` is ignored, and the warnings name every file written outside the export destination; an Export Folder of `../x` and one of `/tmp/x` in the reference app settle it.
  - Guess: Exports from the command line, MCP and the control channel skip an instance whose Export Folder resolves outside the destination folder; the reference app has no such callers, so nothing there settles it.
  - Observe: In font C (m7-export), export with Export Folder `../trial`, then `/tmp/x`: note where the files go.
- [ ] `file.newProject` (M7, handbook, live): New Project
  - Guess: A new project is saved as a native FontCraft project file (`.fontcraftproject`, JSON: the linked font's path relative to the project, the instances in the native format, the export destination); reading and writing `.glyphsproject` waits for a sample file from the reference app.
  - Guess: Project edits are not undo steps: Edit › Undo acts on font documents only; undoing in a project window of the reference app settles it.
  - Guess: An unsaved project is called Untitled Project (then Untitled Project 2, …); the reference app's window title settles it.
  - Observe: Choose File › New Project, link font B (m7-two) and save: keep the `.glyphsproject` file and hand it over (our own font, so it may be read); it is the sample the project reader and writer wait for.
- [ ] `project.linkFont` (M7, handbook, live): Choose
  - Guess: The link is stored relative to the project file when both share a folder tree, else absolute; linking copies the font's instances into the project only when the project has none; saving a project in the reference app settles the path form.
  - Guess: A link that no longer resolves is kept when the project opens and reported as a warning; only export and revert refuse it; opening a project whose font was moved in the reference app settles it.
  - Observe: Choose File › New Project, link font B (m7-two) and save: note in the saved file whether the link is a relative or an absolute path.
- [ ] `project.addInstance` (M7, handbook, live): Add Instance
  - Guess: A new project instance has the defaults of font.instance.add; a variable font setting waits for M9 and is refused until then.
  - Guess: `at` places the new instance in the list (default: the end); the plus button in the reference app adds at the end or after the selection, which observing it settles.
- [ ] `project.revertInstances` (M7, handbook, live): Revert to Font Instances
  - Guess: Reverting reads the font file on disk, not an open copy with unsaved changes; reverting with the font open and edited in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `project.moveInstances` (M7, handbook, live): Move Instances
  - Guess: Several moved instances keep their order and land together, the first at `to` of the list without them; dragging several rows in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `project.duplicateInstances` (M7, handbook, live): Duplicate Instances
  - Guess: A duplicate keeps the original's name and goes right after it unless `to` is given.
  - Guess: With `to`, the copies go together at that position; a copy gets no id of its own.
- [ ] `project.editInstance` (M7, handbook, live): Edit Instance
  - Guess: Edits are checked as Font Info › Exports checks an export of the linked font (its axes and masters), or of a new empty font while no font is linked.
- [ ] `project.setExportDestination` (M7, handbook, live): Export Destination
  - Guess: The destination is stored relative to the project file like the font link, need not exist, and is created by export; saving a project in the reference app settles the stored form.
- [ ] `project.export` (M7, handbook, live): Export
  - Guess: Export refuses a project with unsaved changes as well as one never saved; exporting in the reference app with an unsaved change settles it.
  - Guess: Export reads the font file on disk even when the font is open with unsaved changes; exporting with the font open and edited settles it.
  - Guess: Export builds TrueType outlines in plain files unless asked otherwise, and a project without an active static instance is refused rather than exporting the font's first master; the reference app's export from a project settles both.
  - Guess: Export is refused, writing nothing, when one of its files would replace the linked font (a compiled font in the destination folder); exporting such a project in the reference app settles it.
- [ ] `project.open` (M7, inferred, live): Open Project
  - Guess: A project already open is made active rather than opened twice, as for fonts; a font link that no longer resolves is kept and reported; opening one in the reference app settles both.
- [ ] `project.save` (M7, handbook, live): Save Project
  - Guess: Saving writes only the project file, atomically, and refuses a path that is the linked font; File › Save in a project window runs this command.
- [ ] `project.close` (M7, inferred, live): Close Project
  - Guess: Closing a project with unsaved changes refuses unless `discard` is true, as closing a font does; closing a changed project window in the reference app would settle what it asks.
- [ ] `project.list` (M7, inferred, live): List Projects
  - Guess: A query of ours listing each open project's font link, destination, instances and saved state; the reference app shows the same facts only in each project window.
- [ ] `open.reconstructsFeatures` (behavior, M8, handbook): OpenType layout from a compiled font is turned back into feature code…
  - Observe: Open a compiled OTF exported from F1 (Latin features) with a lookup used by two features and contextual kerning: note whether a prefix named Prefix holds the shared lookup, and what happens to the contextual kerning.
- [ ] `file.importFeatures` (M8, handbook, live): Features…
  - Guess: A class or prefix whose name the font already has gets the first free numeric suffix from 2 (`Upper2`), and the imported code's references to a renamed class follow it; importing into a font with clashing names in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: A feature whose tag the font already has is added as a second entry with that tag, because blocks of one tag add up in the feature-file language and a suffixed tag is not a tag; the same check would settle it.
  - Guess: Each run of top-level statements other than class definitions and feature blocks becomes a prefix named Prefix, and a `# Prefix: name` comment names the prefix after it; importing a file with lookups and language systems in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: Statements the split cannot place (a `useExtension` feature, an `anon` block, a class whose value is not a list) stay in a prefix as written rather than failing the import.
  - Observe: In F1 (Latin features), import a `.fea` file whose class and feature names already exist in the font: note how each clash is resolved.
- [ ] `dialog.importFeatures` (panel, M8, handbook): Import Features
  - Guess: The file is typed as a path, as in the other import dialogs, until the shell has a file chooser limited to .fea files.
- [ ] `file.exportVariable` (M9, handbook, live): Export Variable Fonts
  - Guess: A glyph that has no layer for the origin master does not vary and keeps the origin's empty outline, with a warning; exporting such a font from the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: An intermediate layer that lies outside the axes of a variable font setting (one that disables masters) is left out of that font, with a note; exporting such a setting from the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `variable-ttf.open` (format, M9, inferred): Variable OpenType, TrueType outlines (opened for editing)
  - Guess: A variable font beyond the limits (more than 256 avar points on an axis, more than 2,000,000 layers to make) opens as its default instance with the reason among the warnings rather than being refused; settled by checking what users expect when a huge variable font opens.
  - Guess: A peak becomes a master when every coordinate is -1, 0 or 1, or when at least half of the glyphs with variations use it; any other peak becomes an intermediate layer of the glyphs that use it, on the nearest master. Settled by opening fonts built from sources with known masters and intermediate layers and comparing.
  - Guess: A master takes the name of the named instance at its location, else its axis names and design coordinates; an intermediate layer is named by its coordinates in braces.
- [ ] `cff2.open` (format, M9, inferred): Variable OpenType, CFF2 outlines (opened for editing)
  - Guess: Every region of the CFF2 variation store becomes a master, since the charstrings do not say which glyphs use which region without evaluating them. Settled by opening CFF2 fonts built from sources with intermediate layers and comparing.
- [ ] `cff2.export` (format, M9, docs): Variable OpenType, CFF2 outlines (export)
  - Guess: Manual PostScript hints are left out of a CFF2 font, with a warning, where fontmake would write blended stems; comparing the hinted output of the two would settle whether blended hints are worth writing.
- [ ] `compile.instanceInterpolation` (behavior, M9, docs): Static instances are produced by interpolating masters at the instance location before…
  - Guess: Copied from fontmake's instancer: a glyph with no layer in the origin master is left out of the instance; a glyph whose layer is empty in some masters but drawn at the origin interpolates without the empty ones; outlines are not rounded before the compile; a glyph that does not export and cannot interpolate keeps its nearest layer instead of failing the instance. Settle each with an instance export from the reference app.
- [ ] `compile.instanceLayoutInterpolation` (behavior, M9, docs): fontmake's feature writers interpolate kerning and anchors for instances but not explicit…
  - Guess: A variable font's kerning groups are the font's glyph groups in every master; a UFO master whose own groups differ is read with the glyph groups. A designspace whose masters' groups differ would settle it against fontmake.
  - Guess: When a master's mark rules differ from the origin's (an anchor missing in one master), the mark positions keep the origin's values with a warning instead of failing the export. An export from the reference app of such a font would settle it.
- [ ] `compile.kernGroupReconciliation` (behavior, M9, docs): When masters group glyphs differently for kerning, output classes are the common…
  - Guess: A static instance interpolates kerning as fontmake's instancer does (fontMath): a pair one master lacks takes the value that master's groups give it (the pair, the second side's group, the first side's group, both groups, else zero: the compiled kern lookup's order, where fontMath tries the first side's group before the second); a master read from its own UFO uses that file's groups; a pair that comes out zero is dropped unless one side is a glyph in a group; values are rounded half up. The common refinement of the groups is for variable fonts (M9.9); settle by comparing an instance of a source whose masters group glyphs differently with fontmake.
- [ ] `compile.bracketLayerGlyphs` (behavior, M9, docs): Each alternate (bracket) layer set becomes an extra glyph named after its…
  - Guess: When the font already has a glyph named like an alternate glyph (`a.BRACKET.varAlt01`), the alternate takes the next free number instead of replacing or clashing with it; settle by exporting such a font from the reference app and reading the glyph order.
  - Guess: A kern pair on a glyph with alternate layers is copied to its alternate glyphs the way glyphsLib copies it, not to every combination of alternates as fontc does; settle by exporting a variable font with a pair between two glyphs that both have alternate layers and reading its kerning at a location inside both ranges.
- [ ] `export.autohintCff` (behavior, M10, handbook): With Autohint on and the CFF flavour, the PostScript autohinter runs only…
  - Guess: Zones or stems the autohinter cannot use (overlapping zones, fewer than two BlueValues zones, a direction without stems) make the export autohint without them, with a warning, where otfautohint refuses the font; a reference export of a font with overlapping zones would show what it does.
- [ ] `color.export.fullColorToColrV1` (behavior, M11, docs): Full-colour layers are compiled to COLR version 1 when any of them…
  - Guess: A full-colour Color layer exports as COLR version 1 without any parameter, as both oracles do; the handbook names only SVG for these layers. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font from the reference with no parameters.
  - Observe: Full-colour font: export with no colour parameters and read it with fontTools; note whether COLR version 1, an SVG table or neither is written.
- [ ] `color.export.colrVersionChoice` (behavior, M11, docs): The compiler writes COLR version 0 when every colour glyph is a…
  - Guess: Solid-only full-colour layers export as COLR version 0, as fontc does, not version 1 as fontmake does. Settled by exporting the full-colour test font with only solid fills from the reference.
  - Observe: Full-colour font with the gradient and stroke paths deleted (solid fills only): export and read COLR; note whether it is version 0 or version 1.
- [ ] `type1` (format, M12, docs): Adobe Type 1 (PFB binary, PFA text)
  - Guess: Vertical metrics are measured on the glyphs d, H, x and p (falling back to the FontBBox), since a Type 1 font has no such fields; the reference app's import of a .pfb would settle it.
  - Guess: Ghost hints become TopGhost and BottomGhost hints with place (edge, 0), the edge being y for a top ghost and y + dy for a bottom ghost as tx reports them; the reference app's import of a hinted .pfb would settle it.
  - Guess: Standard stems are named hStem0, hStem1… and vStem0…; the reference app's import of a .pfb would show its names.
  - Guess: A font whose glyphs together hold more than 20,000,000 hint and path operations, or take more than 500,000,000 charstring steps to run, is refused rather than read in part; no limit of the reference readers is documented, and real fonts stay far below both. A font past them in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: A repeated glyph name gets .1, .2…; when the name is too long for its suffix, it is cut to 247 bytes first so the result stays within 255. Importing a font with repeated long names into the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `cff` (format, M12, docs): Bare CFF font program
  - Guess: A font whose glyphs together hold more than 20,000,000 hint and path operations, or take more than 500,000,000 charstring steps to run, is refused rather than read in part; no limit of the reference readers is documented, and real fonts stay far below both. A font past them in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `svg-font` (format, M12, docs): SVG 1.1 font
  - Guess: An open subpath in a glyph's path data is read as closed, since SVG fonts fill every subpath; no reference reader was available to settle it.
  - Guess: A glyph without glyph-name is named from its characters through the glyph database (uniXXXX when it cannot be loaded), ligatures joined with underscores; a reference importer of SVG fonts would settle it.
  - Guess: Only the first missing-glyph element is read, as .notdef; later ones are left out with a warning. A reference importer of SVG fonts would settle it.
  - Guess: A repeated glyph name gets .1, .2…; when the name is too long for its suffix, it is cut to 247 bytes first so the result stays within 255. Importing a font with repeated long names into the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `proof-html` (format, M12, inferred): HTML specimen
  - Guess: The default sample is an English pangram with digits when the font maps all of its characters, else the first 64 characters of the character set. Settled by the owner.
- [ ] `glyphsproject` (format, M13, handbook): Glyphs project
  - Observe: Make a Project from the instances test font (two masters, three instances, one with a custom parameter), set an export destination, save it, and send back the `.glyphsproject` file: its keys show where the instances, the link to the source and the destination are stored.
- [ ] `ufo1` (format, M13, docs): Unified Font Object 1
  - Guess: A font-info value that ufoLib cannot convert (an unknown `fontStyle`, `widthName` or `msCharSet`, or a value of the wrong type) is dropped and the file opens; ufoLib and norad refuse the file instead. A UFO 1 from the tools of that time with such a value would settle whether refusing is better.
  - Guess: The feature code is joined as defcon joins it (a line break between the classes and each feature, tags sorted when the lib has no order); norad leaves out the line breaks between features. Both texts compile the same; a UFO 1 whose features only work with one of the two joins would settle it.
  - Guess: A tag the feature order lists twice is written once, so a crafted order cannot multiply the feature code; defcon writes it each time.
  - Guess: Font-info keys a UFO 1 does not define are dropped, as ufoLib drops them, so the saved UFO 3 holds only valid keys.
- [ ] `pfm` (format, M13, docs): Printer Font Metrics (Windows)
  - Guess: A word before the pair list equal to `etmKernPairs` is a count and is skipped (and gives the count when `etmKernPairs` is 0); the DDK describes no such word, but files made for PostScript printers are said to have it. A PFM file from a font vendor would settle it.
  - Guess: Character codes of a font whose `dfCharSet` is not 2 (symbol) are read as Windows-1252; a symbol font's code c is looked up as U+F000 + c, then as c. PFM files of symbol fonts from a vendor would settle it.
  - Guess: Kerning amounts are in `etmMasterUnits` per em (1,000 when the field is 0, as the DDK says PostScript drivers assume) and are scaled to the font's units per em, rounded half up.
- [ ] `sfd` (format, M13, docs): FontForge Spline Font Database
  - Guess: A contour is closed when its last point equals its first and open otherwise; the page does not say how an open contour is written. A file from the originating editor with an open path would settle it.
  - Guess: In a quadratic layer a `c` line is read as a quadratic segment when both controls are the same point or are exactly two thirds of the way to one point; the page does not say how quadratic splines are written. A quadratic file from the editor would settle it.
  - Guess: An on-curve point flagged 0x80 that lies halfway between two control points is dropped, so runs of quadratic control points come back. A TrueType-derived file from the editor would settle it.
  - Guess: The point flags of a closed contour's start come from its `m` line, not from the line that returns to it. A file with a smooth start point would settle it.
  - Guess: `Refer` and `Kerns2` glyph numbers are the third number of a glyph's `Encoding` line (its original position), or its position in the file when that is missing; a reference that matches no glyph falls back to its code point. Files with reordered glyphs would settle it.
  - Guess: A glyph without a `Width` line is as wide as the em. A file with such a glyph would settle it.
  - Guess: Anchor names follow the reference's convention: the class name for base and base-mark anchors, `_` before it for marks, `_1`, `_2`… after it for ligature components, `entry` and `exit` for cursive anchors. The page names only the classes.
  - Guess: Pair positioning is lookup type 258 (0x102), read from the page's example; subtables no lookup declares count as kerning. A file with several lookup types would settle it.
  - Guess: Glyph pairs take precedence over class pairs, and an earlier class table over a later one, as in the model; in OpenType the order of subtables decides. A file with overlapping subtables would settle it.
  - Guess: A kerning class becomes a group named after its first glyph; a glyph already in a group on that side kerns by name instead. Files with a glyph in classes of two tables would settle it.
  - Guess: Cap height and x-height, which the format does not store, are the tops of H and x, or 70% and 50% of the em; overshoots are zero.
  - Guess: Hhea, typo and win values marked as offsets from a computed value are not kept, because the page does not say how the value is computed.
  - Guess: The digits after the point in `Version` are thousandths (`1.5` is version 1.500).
  - Guess: A TrueType instruction block (`TtTable`, `TtInstrs`) ends at a line starting `EndTt` or at the first line that is neither a mnemonic nor numbers; the page names no end line.
  - Guess: A glyph layer the font does not declare (the multi-layered Type 3 form) is merged into the master layer.
  - Guess: A glyph listed in more than one class on one side of a class kerning table belongs to the first of them; the later listings are skipped and reported. A table from the originating editor with such a repeat would settle which class it keeps.
- [ ] `eps-outline` (format, M13, handbook): Encapsulated PostScript artwork (glyph outline import)
  - Guess: Unscaled placement keeps one point per unit and puts the top edge of the bounding box (else of the drawing) at the given top, matching how SVG files are placed; importing an EPS of known geometry in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: Paths that are only used to clip, or ended without painting, are left out and counted as dropped; the reference may import every path, which importing a file with a clipping path and an unpainted path would settle.
  - Guess: Arcs (arc, arcn, arct), text and images make the file fail with a message rather than being skipped; an EPS with an arc or a text object imported in the reference app would show whether it skips them or converts them.
  - Guess: A path filled inside gsave/grestore and stroked again afterwards is imported once; a reference import of such a file would settle it.
  - Observe: Import Outlines with an EPS of our own that draws a 100 × 100 point square at (50, 50) in a 0 0 200 200 bounding box: note where the square lands relative to the baseline and whether it is scaled.
  - Observe: Import Outlines with an EPS that clips a filled triangle by a rectangle and also has an unpainted path: note which of the three paths arrive.
  - Observe: Import Outlines with an EPS that uses `arc` and one that contains text: note whether each is refused, skipped or converted.
- [ ] `io.watchSourceOnDisk` (behavior, M13, docs): While a file-per-glyph source is open, the application watches it for modification…
  - Guess: Every source with a file is watched, single-file sources (.glyphs, .fontcraft, .designspace) and compiled fonts included, not only file-per-glyph sources; the manual names UFOs only.
  - Guess: A file that is only touched (same content) refreshes the stamp without a prompt; touching an open UFO in the reference settles it.
  - Guess: A document whose file was deleted on disk raises no prompt; the next save writes the file again. Deleting an open UFO in the reference settles it.
- [ ] `file.exportWithProfiles` (M13, docs, live): Export Fonts As…
  - Guess: Without `fonts` the active document is exported, not every open one; the reference's Export Fonts As dialog would settle the default.
  - Guess: A profile's subfolder may not climb out of the destination with `..`, for any caller; the reference's profile settings would settle whether it can.
  - Guess: Two profiles that would write the same file refuse the whole export instead of keeping one; the reference's behaviour with two profiles sharing a folder would settle it.
- [ ] `file.saveExportProfile` (M13, docs, live): Save Export Profile
  - Guess: A copy made with basedOn writes into a subfolder named after the copy unless `settings` names a folder; the reference's Duplicate would settle it.
- [ ] `glyph.exportArtwork` (M13, docs, live): Export Artwork…
  - Guess: SVG uses the io-svg writer, PNG the render crate and PDF a minimal writer of our own; FontLab's options beyond the three formats are not followed.
  - Guess: A picture spans the advance width and the master's descender to ascender, widened to the outline where it reaches further, so pictures of one font line up; exporting a few glyphs in the reference would settle the frame.
  - Guess: SVG and PDF use one user unit or point per font unit (a PDF is scaled down to at most 14400 points a side); PNG is black on a transparent background at 256 pixels per em by default.
  - Guess: Characters a file name cannot hold become `_`, and names that differ only in case get `_2`, `_3`; the reference's file names for such glyphs would settle it.
  - Guess: Without `glyphs` every glyph of the font is exported; the shell passes the selection.
- [ ] `file.revertGlyph` (M13, docs, live): Revert Glyph
  - Guess: Without glyphs, every glyph that differs from the saved file is reverted; glyphs added since the save are left alone and naming one is refused. Reverting a glyph added since the save in the secondary reference would settle it.
  - Guess: With layers, a layer added since the save is removed and a deleted one is put back. The secondary reference reverts whole glyphs only, so nothing there settles it.
- [ ] `file.importMetricsFile` (M13, docs, live): Import Kerning from Metrics File…
  - Guess: `replace` gives a pair the master already has the file's value and keeps every other pair; `addMissing` adds only pairs the master lacks; the reference documentation does not say what happens to existing pairs. Merging a file into a font that already kerns one of its pairs in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: Without `masters`, the pairs go into the first master only, as other import commands do; the reference has one master per font.
  - Guess: Pairs that name a glyph (AFM) or a character code (PFM) the font lacks are left out and listed, not stored; importing a file with such a pair in the reference app would settle it.
  - Guess: Ligature data in an AFM file is not imported: ligature features come from glyph names; the reference merges it into its ligature tables.
- [ ] `file.reviewExternalChanges` (M13, docs, live): Review External Changes
  - Guess: The document's own unsaved edits are kept and never listed, so Update All merges rather than reloads; the manual speaks of taking the incoming change, and a document with unsaved edits in the reference settles whether it keeps them.
  - Guess: Font-level data is grouped into nine parts (info, axes, masters, instances, kerning, features, groups, glyph order, other), each accepted whole; the manual mentions font-level data without saying how finely it is split.
  - Guess: Glyph data apart from the layers (code points, export, kerning groups, metadata) is one item per glyph, beside one item per changed layer; changing a glyph's code point in a UFO while it is open in the reference settles how it is listed.
- [ ] `dialog.exportProfiles` (panel, M13, docs): Export Profiles
  - Guess: The built-in profiles are OpenType PS (CFF, plain), OpenType TT (TrueType, plain) and Web (TrueType, WOFF2 and WOFF), each writing into a subfolder named after it; the reference's full built-in list would settle it.
  - Guess: There is no tick for which profiles the export dialog shows: Export Fonts As lists every profile; the reference's dialog would settle whether hidden profiles are needed.
- [ ] `dialog.externalChanges` (panel, M13, docs): External Changes
  - Guess: Every item starts ticked, so Accept Selected with no change of the ticks takes everything; the reference's manual does not say which state the ticks start in, and opening the window there settles it.
  - Guess: The stored-text view on double-click shows the glyph in FontCraft's native JSON form rather than as a .glif file, since the comparison is between models; the reference shows the file text, and a file-per-glyph text view would follow it more closely.

### Settings and the window (12)

- [ ] `addons.repo.httpBasicAuth` (behavior, M13, handbook): An alternate repository URL may require a user name and password, sent…
  - Guess: Credentials are taken from the URL itself and stored with the setting, not in the operating system's keychain; the reference's storage is not described.
- [ ] `settings.addons` (panel, M13, handbook): Addons
  - Guess: The section is called Extensions, because FontCraft has no add-on kinds that need Python; the reference calls it Addons.
  - Observe: Screenshot Settings › Addons in the trial and list its controls in order, with defaults.
- [ ] `addons.useSystemConsoleForScriptOutput` (setting, M13, handbook): Use system console for script output
  - Guess: With the setting on, the log still shows in the Command Console as well as on standard error, where the handbook says the reference sends it to the system console instead; keeping both loses nothing and the switch stays off by default.
- [ ] `window.macroPanel` (M13, inferred, live): Macro Panel
  - Guess: A run-style line with several commands runs them as one transaction and one undo step, and may name engine commands only, so a failing line changes nothing; the command-line tool runs them one by one and simply does not save after a failure.
  - Guess: Clear empties the console's pane but keeps the session's command log, which other readers use; the reference's clear behaviour is not described.
  - Guess: The history lives for the session only and is not saved between launches; watching whether the reference keeps typed text after a relaunch would settle it.
  - Observe: Open Window › Macro Panel: screenshot it, note how code is run (a button, a shortcut, both), whether it has tabs, and whether the typed text is still there after closing and reopening the panel and after relaunching.
- [ ] `window.macroPanel` (panel, M13, inferred): Macro Panel
  - Guess: The console's input is one line with a history, not a multi-line editor, because commands are one line of JSON; the reference's panel layout is not described (open question).
  - Guess: Tab takes the first completion suggestion and Up and Down stay with the history, because the history is the row's documented use of those keys; nothing describes completion keys in the reference.
- [ ] `app.quit` (M14, memory): Quit FontCraft
  - Open (recalled, not confirmed): Quits after asking about each document with unsaved changes. Background saves and exports finish first.
- [ ] `sharing.fontcraftEquivalent` (behavior, M14, inferred): FontCraft has no companion app planned; the same need can be met…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): FontCraft has no companion app planned; the same need can be met by a read-only preview stream over the control channel that any browser on the network can open after confirmation on the host.
- [ ] `shortcuts.oneRegistry` (behavior, M14, inferred): FontCraft derives the action list from the command registry: every command with…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): FontCraft derives the action list from the command registry: every command with a label is listed under its menu or noun prefix, and the stored override map is keyed by command id.
- [ ] `settings.resetWarnings` (M14, memory): Reset Warnings
  - Open (recalled, not confirmed): Makes every warning dialog that was silenced with a do-not-show-again choice appear again. This chapter does not mention it; recalled from earlier versions of the reference and to be confirmed.
- [ ] `settings.setSampleStrings` (M14, inferred): Set Sample Strings
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Replaces the strings of one group; the UI equivalent is editing the text field. Proposed for headless use.
- [ ] `settings.resetShortcut` (M14, inferred): Reset Shortcut
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Drops the user's override for one action, or for all when no id is given, restoring built-in shortcuts. Not described in the source; proposed because Customized filtering implies a way back.
- [ ] `settings.listShortcuts` (M14, inferred): List Shortcuts
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Read-only query returning each action with its section, default shortcut and current shortcut. Proposed for agents; equals the content of the list.

### Scripts, plug-ins and URLs (61)

- [ ] `app.launchWithoutPlugins` (behavior, M13, handbook): Holding Option and Shift while the application starts launches it with every…
  - Observe: Hold Option and Shift while launching: note any dialog or message, and whether plug-ins can be turned back on without relaunching.
- [ ] `filter.menu.pluginFiltersListed` (behavior, M13, handbook): Filters installed through the Plugin Manager appear in the Filter menu next…
  - Observe: Install a free filter plug-in from the Plugin Manager and relaunch: note where it appears in the Filter menu (below a separator, sorted with the built-in filters, or in a submenu) and whether its item ends with an ellipsis.
- [ ] `url.scheme` (behavior, M13, inferred): The reference answers to two URL schemes, one generic and one tied…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The reference answers to two URL schemes, one generic and one tied to a major version, so web pages and other apps can open it. FontCraft registers `fontcraft://`.
- [ ] `url.show` (behavior, M13, inferred): `fontcraft://show/?...` opens files and adds a new tab listing given glyph layers;…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): `fontcraft://show/?...` opens files and adds a new tab listing given glyph layers; it mirrors the reference's show path and takes the query parameters path, glyph, production and layer.
  - Observe: In the trial, open `glyphsapp://show/?path=<test font>&glyph=A&glyph=B` from a browser or Terminal (`open '…'`): note whether a new tab opens with A and B on the main master, and whether the window comes to the front.
- [ ] `url.show.path` (behavior, M13, inferred): `path` is an absolute file path and comes first
  - Guess: A path that is empty, relative, a network share, not a file or folder, or that cannot be opened is skipped with a warning together with its glyphs, and the rest of the URL is still handled; the observe step below settles it.
  - Observe: Open a show URL whose path names a file that does not exist, and one whose path is not absolute: note any message and whether the rest of the URL is still processed.
- [ ] `url.show.glyph` (behavior, M13, inferred): `glyph` adds a glyph to the current file's list by its glyph…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): `glyph` adds a glyph to the current file's list by its glyph name; glyphs appear in the tab in URL order.
  - Observe: Open a show URL naming a glyph the font lacks between two that exist: note whether the tab shows the two, shows a placeholder, or nothing opens.
- [ ] `url.show.production` (behavior, M13, inferred): `production` adds a glyph by its production name (e.g
  - Guess: A glyph without a production name of its own is found by its glyph name; a show URL with production=A in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `url.show.layer` (behavior, M13, inferred): `layer` follows a glyph and selects one of its layers by layer…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): `layer` follows a glyph and selects one of its layers by layer ID; repeating it shows several layers of the same glyph. A master layer's ID equals the master ID.
  - Observe: Open a show URL with a layer id that does not belong to the glyph: note what the tab shows for that glyph.
- [ ] `url.show.defaultLayer` (behavior, M13, inferred): A glyph with no layer parameter is shown on the layer of…
  - Guess: The main master is the font's first master; a font whose main master is not the first, opened by a show URL in the reference app, would settle it.
- [ ] `url.show.alreadyOpen` (behavior, M13, inferred): A file that is already open is not opened a second time;…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): A file that is already open is not opened a second time; it only gets the new tab.
  - Observe: With the test font open and edited but unsaved, open a show URL naming it: confirm no second window opens and the edits are kept.
- [ ] `url.show.encoding` (behavior, M13, inferred): Query values are percent-decoded as UTF-8, and unknown glyphs or layer IDs…
  - Guess: Query values are percent-decoded as UTF-8 and unknown glyphs or layer ids are skipped with a warning; the reference does not say.
  - Guess: A `+` stays a plus sign rather than a space, and a URL holding raw spaces, control characters or non-ASCII characters is refused; a show URL with `+` in a glyph name in the reference app would settle the first.
  - Guess: A `%` not followed by two hexadecimal digits, bytes that are not UTF-8 and encoded control characters refuse the whole URL; unknown parameters are skipped with a warning.
  - Observe: Open a show URL whose path contains a space written as %20 and a glyph name with a non-ASCII character percent-encoded: note whether both work.
- [ ] `url.safety` (behavior, M13, inferred): URL handling is limited to opening and showing; it never runs arbitrary…
  - Guess: URLs only open and show; any other path after `fontcraft://` is refused, so a web page cannot run commands; the reference documents only `show`.
  - Guess: A URL with a fragment, user information or anything after `show/` other than the query is refused; device files and network shares are not opened.
- [ ] `url.open` (M13, inferred, live): Open URL
  - Guess: The command fails, changing nothing, only when the URL is malformed, over a limit or opens no file at all; a file that cannot be opened is skipped with a warning and the rest still open. Opening a show URL with one missing file and one existing file in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `url.show` (M13, inferred, live): Show Glyphs in New Tab
  - Guess: A tab holds one layer per glyph, so a glyph listed in several layers appears once per listing but every copy draws in the first layer given, with a warning; the reference shows each listed layer. Per-item layers in tabs would remove the difference.
  - Guess: The new tab draws in the master of its first listed layer. Opening a show URL whose first glyph names a second master's layer in the reference app would settle it.
- [ ] `extensions.twoFamilies` (behavior, M13, inferred): The reference is extended by scripts (run on demand from a menu)…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The reference is extended by scripts (run on demand from a menu) and plug-ins (loaded at launch, adding menu entries, drawing, tools, palettes or formats); many are free through the Plugin Manager, others are sold elsewhere, and users can write their own. FontCraft: two layers replace both: the command registry driven from outside (CLI, JSON control channel, MCP) for automation, and sandboxed WebAssembly modules for code that must run inside the app.
- [ ] `pluginManager.relaunchForPluginsAndModules` (behavior, M13, inferred): A plug-in or module installed during a session is usable only once…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): A plug-in or module installed during a session is usable only once the app has been relaunched. FontCraft: modules are loaded and unloaded while the app runs, so installing never needs a relaunch; a module is compiled when installed and instantiated fresh for each run.
- [ ] `pluginManager.scriptsLoadWithoutRelaunch` (behavior, M13, inferred): Newly installed scripts appear without relaunching once the Script menu is reloaded…
  - Guess: Folders are checked by comparing file names, sizes and modification times when the window regains focus and every 5 seconds, rather than with an operating-system file watcher, so the same code works on every platform; a reference behaviour is not involved.
- [ ] `pluginManager.automaticUpdates` (behavior, M13, inferred): Installed extensions are updated to newer versions automatically
  - Guess: Updates are offered, not installed silently, unless the user turns automatic updates on per index; the reference installs them automatically and does not say whether that can be turned off (open question in docs/spec/17-extensions.md).
  - Observe: In Settings › Addons and the Plugin Manager, look for any switch that turns automatic extension updates off, globally or per extension.
- [ ] `pluginManager.repositories` (behavior, M13, inferred): The catalogue is the main public repository plus any alternate repositories entered…
  - Guess: When two indexes list the same id, the index listed first in the settings wins; the reference does not say how it merges entries.
- [ ] `pluginManager.submission` (behavior, M13, inferred): New entries reach the main catalogue through a pull request on the…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): New entries reach the main catalogue through a pull request on the public plug-in repository or by contacting the vendor. FontCraft: an index is a JSON file anyone can host, as a local file or over HTTPS; there is no public main index (docs/plans/M13.md § 7 question 5), so entries reach users through the indexes their vendors publish.
- [ ] `script.scope` (behavior, M13, inferred): Scripts are Python files that can automate every part of the app…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Scripts are Python files that can automate every part of the app through its Python API. FontCraft: everything a script could do is a command, so a script is either a macro (a stored list of command invocations with parameters, the same file as an action set) or an external program in any language calling the CLI, control channel or MCP server.
- [ ] `script.menu.mirrorsFolder` (behavior, M13, inferred): The Script menu is a picture of the Scripts folder: one item…
  - Guess: Script menu items are sorted by title, case-insensitively and with numbers in numeric order, folders before items; the handbook does not give the order.
  - Observe: Put three scripts in the Scripts folder: `b.py` titled Alpha, `a.py` titled Zulu and `c.py` without a MenuTitle, plus a subfolder `Tools` with one script; reload and record the order of items and submenus, and what the untitled script is called.
- [ ] `script.menu.subfolders` (behavior, M13, inferred): Subfolders of the Scripts folder become submenus, which is how scripts are…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Subfolders of the Scripts folder become submenus, which is how scripts are grouped. FontCraft: same rule for the macros folder.
  - Observe: Give two scripts in different subfolders the same MenuTitle, and two in one folder the same MenuTitle: note how the menu shows each clash.
- [ ] `script.menu.tooltip` (behavior, M13, inferred): Hovering briefly over a script in the menu shows the script's short…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Hovering briefly over a script in the menu shows the script's short description, when it has one. FontCraft: a macro's description field is shown as the tooltip and returned as the command's params doc.
  - Observe: Rest the pointer on a script item that has a __doc__ string and time how long the tooltip takes to appear; check whether a multi-line description keeps its line breaks.
- [ ] `script.shortcutAssignable` (behavior, M13, inferred): A script can be given a keyboard shortcut in Settings › Shortcuts
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): A script can be given a keyboard shortcut in Settings › Shortcuts. FontCraft: macros are commands, so they appear in the shortcut editor like any other.
- [ ] `script.requiresModules` (behavior, M13, inferred): Some scripts only work after particular modules have been installed from the…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Some scripts only work after particular modules have been installed from the Modules tab. FontCraft: a macro calls only commands; when it calls a filter, reporter or check module, its `requires` list names the module ids, the Extension Manager installs them with it, and running it without them refuses before any step runs.
- [ ] `script.file.pyExtension` (behavior, M13, inferred): A script is a file ending in .py placed in the Scripts…
  - Guess: Macro files end in `.fcmacro` and hold JSON; nothing in the reference decides it (the format is ours, open decision D8).
- [ ] `script.file.menuTitleHeader` (behavior, M13, inferred): The menu title comes from a comment at the top of the…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The menu title comes from a comment at the top of the file of the form '# MenuTitle: Script Name'. FontCraft: the macro's `title` field, falling back to the file name without its extension.
- [ ] `script.file.docString` (behavior, M13, inferred): The tooltip text is the script's __doc__ string assigned below the MenuTitle…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The tooltip text is the script's __doc__ string assigned below the MenuTitle line; it may be a triple-quoted string spanning several lines. FontCraft: the macro's `description` field, which may hold line breaks; it is the menu tooltip and the description agents read.
- [ ] `script.importExistingPython` (behavior, M13, inferred): Existing scripts are Python against the reference's API and cannot run elsewhere
  - Guess: The repository ships no Python client package, only documented examples, because the code base is Rust only; nothing in the reference decides it.
- [ ] `plugin.kind.reporter` (behavior, M13, inferred): Reporter plug-ins (.glyphsReporter) draw extra information in Edit View and each gets…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Reporter plug-ins (.glyphsReporter) draw extra information in Edit View and each gets a Show … entry at the foot of the View menu that switches it. FontCraft: a reporter module receives one layer as read-only JSON with the view scale and whether the glyph is the active one, and returns drawing primitives (paths, lines, circles, text) for the foreground and background; results are cached per layer revision and scale. Each installed reporter gets a Show entry at the foot of the View menu (view.showReporter).
- [ ] `plugin.kind.filter` (behavior, M13, inferred): Filter plug-ins (.glyphsFilter) process glyph layers and are listed in the Filter…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Filter plug-ins (.glyphsFilter) process glyph layers and are listed in the Filter menu (and usable as export-time filter parameters). FontCraft: a filter module receives one layer as JSON with its parameters and returns the layer's new paths, components and anchors; plugin.runFilter calls it once per target layer and applies all results as one undo step. Installed filters are listed in the Filter menu and can be named in the Filter export parameter.
- [ ] `plugin.kind.palette` (behavior, M13, inferred): Palette plug-ins (.glyphsPalette) add sections to the Palette sidebar (Window › Palette,…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Palette plug-ins (.glyphsPalette) add sections to the Palette sidebar (Window › Palette, Cmd+Shift+P). FontCraft: no module kind runs interface code; a filter module that declares parameters can be shown as a Palette section holding its parameter form and an Apply button, drawn by the app.
- [ ] `plugin.loadedAtLaunch` (behavior, M13, inferred): Plug-ins are loaded once when the app starts, hence the relaunch after…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Plug-ins are loaded once when the app starts, hence the relaunch after installing. FontCraft: modules in the modules folder are validated and compiled at launch and whenever the folder changes; each run uses a fresh instance, so nothing needs a relaunch.
- [ ] `plugin.pluginsFolder` (behavior, M13, inferred): Installed plug-ins live in a Plugins folder that sits beside the Scripts…
  - Guess: The folders are `Macros` and `Modules` in the per-user application data folder; the reference's folder names and location are not given in the handbook (open question).
- [ ] `plugin.noManualMove` (behavior, M13, inferred): Plug-ins must not be copied into the Plugins folder by hand because…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Plug-ins must not be copied into the Plugins folder by hand because that conflicts with the operating system's security checks; installation has to go through the app. FontCraft: copying a module into the Modules folder is allowed; a module the app has not seen before is listed as new and stays off until the user turns it on in the Extension Manager or runs plugin.installFromFile on it.
- [ ] `plugin.uninstall` (behavior, M13, inferred): Plug-ins from the manager are removed there; any plug-in can also be…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Plug-ins from the manager are removed there; any plug-in can also be removed by deleting its file in the Plugins folder. FontCraft: both ways work; deleting the file removes the module's menu entries at the next folder check.
  - Observe: Install a plug-in by dropping it on the Dock icon, then open the Plugin Manager: note whether it is listed as installed and whether its Remove button works.
- [ ] `sdk.templates` (behavior, M13, inferred): The open-source Glyphs SDK supplies plug-in templates in two languages, Python and…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The open-source Glyphs SDK supplies plug-in templates in two languages, Python and Objective-C, and plug-ins start from those. FontCraft: a module template in Rust for each module kind (filter, reporter, check) and the ABI documentation, so any language that compiles to WebAssembly can be used.
- [ ] `api.python` (behavior, M13, inferred): A documented Python API exposes the application and its font objects to…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): A documented Python API exposes the application and its font objects to scripts and plug-ins; the chapter refers to that documentation rather than listing the API. FontCraft: the API is the command registry, documented from the same metadata (id, label, parameters, result) for the CLI, the control channel, MCP and macros; modules see the layer JSON of the native format.
- [ ] `api.externalInterop` (behavior, M13, inferred): The SDK also provides ways for a separate application to work with…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The SDK also provides ways for a separate application to work with the running app. FontCraft: this is the primary model: the JSON control channel and the MCP server expose every command to other processes, with the same parameters as the UI.
- [ ] `extensions.authoringDocs` (behavior, M13, inferred): Authors are pointed to API references, a scripting tutorial series, a plug-in…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Authors are pointed to API references, a scripting tutorial series, a plug-in writing tutorial, existing scripts as examples and the vendor's forum. FontCraft: generated command reference, module SDK guide with worked examples, and a porting table from common reference-API tasks to commands.
- [ ] `extensions.sandboxCapabilities` (behavior, M13, inferred): The chapter mentions no permission model for extensions beyond the operating system's…
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The chapter mentions no permission model for extensions beyond the operating system's check on installed plug-ins. FontCraft: a module may import nothing (no files, network, clock, randomness or interface) and sees only the JSON passed to it; each run has an instruction budget, a memory cap and a time limit, and a module that breaks a limit fails with a message and changes nothing. ABI version 1 grants no capabilities; the manifest's `capabilities` list must be empty.
- [ ] `window.pluginManager` (M13, inferred): Plugin Manager
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Opens the Plugin Manager window, the catalogue of installable scripts, plug-ins and modules. FontCraft: opens an Extension Manager listing WebAssembly modules and command macros from the registry index; every action in it is also a command.
  - Observe: Open Window › Plugin Manager in the trial: note the window's default size, whether it is a separate window or a sheet, which filter buttons are selected at first, and whether the Window menu item has a shortcut.
- [ ] `pluginManager.filterByInstallState` (M13, inferred): All / Installed / Not Installed
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Restricts the catalogue by whether an extension is installed. FontCraft: same filter in the Extension Manager, also available as an argument of the listing query.
- [ ] `pluginManager.filterByType` (M13, inferred): Plugins / Scripts / Modules
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Shows one extension type at a time: plug-ins, scripts or modules. FontCraft: Filters, Reporters, Checks or Macros; there is no modules type because each WebAssembly module is self-contained.
- [ ] `pluginManager.search` (M13, inferred): Search
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Filters the catalogue by a keyword matched against extension name, description and vendor name. FontCraft: same three fields are searched.
  - Observe: Type part of a vendor name into the Plugin Manager's search field: confirm entries match on vendor, and note whether matching is case-insensitive and whether it matches inside words.
- [ ] `pluginManager.install` (M13, inferred): Install
  - Guess: An install from an index entry whose checksum does not match the downloaded bytes is refused and nothing is written; the reference's handling of a failed download is not documented.
- [ ] `pluginManager.remove` (M13, inferred): Remove
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Uninstalls an extension that was installed through the manager. FontCraft: unloads the module or forgets the macro, removes its menu entries and moves its file out of the folder; documents that use a removed filter in an export parameter report it at export.
- [ ] `pluginManager.enlargePreview` (M13, inferred): Enlarge Preview
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Clicking an entry's preview image shows it at full size. FontCraft: same, as a view-only action.
- [ ] `pluginManager.openWebsite` (M13, inferred): Open Website
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): The grey link under an entry's description opens the extension's website. FontCraft: same, opening the URL from the registry entry in the system browser.
- [ ] `pluginManager.openDonationPage` (M13, inferred): Donate
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): Entries whose vendor accepts donations show PayPal or Ko-fi icons that open the donation page. FontCraft: one generic `funding` link per index entry, shown as a Donate button and opened in the system browser.
- [ ] `script.run` (M13, inferred, live): (script name)
  - Guess: A macro runs as one undo step that is undone as a whole, like command.batch; the reference undoes script edits per glyph and does not say how a script's changes group.
  - Guess: One top-level macro run executes at most 1,000,000 steps, the steps of the macros it runs included, and reads at most 256 MiB of macro files; past either it fails as a whole. Our own limit; no source describes one for scripts in the reference.
- [ ] `script.rerunLast` (M13, inferred, live): Re-run Last Script
  - Guess: The label of the item at the foot of the Script menu is Re-run Last Script followed by the script's title; the handbook gives no label.
  - Observe: Run any script from the Script menu, then open the Script menu again: copy the exact label of the last item and confirm Cmd+Alt+R runs it.
- [ ] `script.openScriptsFolder` (M13, inferred, live): Open Scripts Folder
  - Guess: The macros folder is created when missing and its path returned, and sessions without one (headless runs, the web app) have the command disabled; the reference app's behaviour when the Scripts folder is missing would settle the first part.
  - Observe: Choose Script › Open Scripts Folder and note the folder's full path, and whether a Plugins folder sits beside it.
- [ ] `script.reloadScripts` (M13, inferred, live): Reload Scripts
  - Guess: It is always shown in the Script menu and returns the new list, since the folder is also watched; files that cannot be read stay listed with their error. Holding Option over the reference app's Script menu settles only where the item shows.
  - Observe: Open the Script menu without and then with Option held: note whether Reload Scripts replaces Open Scripts Folder or is added below it, and where it sits in the menu.
- [ ] `plugin.installFromFile` (M13, inferred): Install Plug-in from File
  - Open (our design, not confirmed): A plug-in outside the catalogue is installed by dropping its bundle on the app's Dock icon; the app moves it into the Plugins folder. FontCraft: dropping a `.wasm` or `.fcmacro` file on the window, opening it with File › Open, or calling this command with a path validates it (manifest, ABI version, no imports, limits) and copies it into its folder; on the web the file's bytes are stored in the browser.
- [ ] `view.showReporter` (M13, inferred): Show (reporter name)
  - Guess: Reporter entries are listed at the foot of the View menu in the order of their names, below a separator; the handbook does not give the order.
  - Observe: Install two reporter plug-ins from the Plugin Manager, relaunch and open the View menu: note where their Show entries are, their order, and whether they have check marks.
- [ ] `window.pluginManager` (panel, M13, inferred): Plugin Manager
  - Guess: The type buttons are Filters, Reporters, Checks and Macros in place of Plugins, Scripts and Modules, because FontCraft has no Python modules; settled by nothing in the reference (our own extension model, decision 0005).
  - Observe: Screenshot the Plugin Manager with All and Plugins selected and an entry that has a preview image and donation icons: record the order of title, description, link, image and buttons in an entry.
- [ ] `script.runActionSet` (M13, docs, live): Run Action Set
  - Guess: Action sets and macros are one file format and one command; FontLab keeps action sets in its Actions folder apart from scripts, a split FontCraft does not copy.
- [ ] `script.saveActionSet` (M13, docs, live): Save Action Set
  - Guess: An existing file is replaced only when `replace` is true, otherwise the save is refused; FontLab's documentation does not say what saving over an existing set does.
- [ ] `dialog.actions` (panel, M13, docs): Actions
  - Guess: Each action's parameter form is the command's own dialog when it has one, and otherwise a JSON editor checked against the command's params doc; FontLab's dialog lists only its own actions, each with a form.

### Elsewhere (19)

- [ ] `audit.glyphSelectionSyntax` (behavior, M12, docs): Wherever a glyph list is accepted on the command line, entries may…
  - Guess: A piece that is a glyph name is read as a name even when it holds a hyphen, and `/N` names the glyph `cidNNNNN` (five digits) before glyph index N; a CID-keyed source in the reference tool would settle it.
- [ ] `audit.familyGrouping` (behavior, M12, docs): Family checks work on two groupings: style-linked groups, fonts with the same…
  - Guess: On a compiled font without its source the style comes from name IDs 2 and 17 and the fsSelection bits, never from the file name; only check.files.canonicalFileName reads the file name (21-qa.md question 9).
- [ ] `audit.validatorKnownIssues` (behavior, M12, docs): Findings imported from an external validator are filtered through a list of…
  - Guess: W2106 (OS/2 version below 3) is treated as wrong for OS/2 version 2 and later, and a ssNN tag counts as registered for 01 to 20 and cvNN for 01 to 99; a run of the validator on fonts with those values would settle it.
- [ ] `audit.exitStatus` (behavior, M12, docs): On the command line the check command exits with 0 when no…
  - Guess: Results hidden by --min-severity still make the exit status 1 when they reach --fail-on; the reference tools have no separate display threshold, so nothing settles it.
- [ ] `audit.profile.save` (M12, inferred, live): Save Audit Profile
  - Guess: Without store the profile goes to the user's settings rather than the project; a user report would settle which store people expect by default.
- [ ] `audit.profile.delete` (M12, inferred, live): Delete Audit Profile
  - Guess: Deleting a profile that another profile of its store includes is refused rather than removing the include; a user report would settle it.
- [ ] `audit.profile.setDefault` (M12, inferred, live): Set Default Audit Profile
  - Guess: target defaults to the document, so a source carries its own default; a user report would settle whether the application default is expected instead.
- [ ] `audit.ignoreIssue` (M12, inferred, live): Ignore Issue
  - Guess: An entry made from a result keeps table-level keys but not outline addresses, which change with every edit; a user report would settle whether point-level entries are wanted.
- [ ] `audit.unignoreIssue` (M12, inferred, live): Stop Ignoring Issue
  - Guess: A call that matches no entry is refused rather than doing nothing, so a mistyped check id is noticed.
- [ ] `audit.listIgnored` (M12, inferred, live): List Ignored Issues
  - Guess: Whether an issue still occurs is judged only against the last kept run, with null when that run did not include the check, rather than running the check again.
- [ ] `audit.export` (M12, docs, live): Export Audit Report
  - Guess: Without `format`, the format follows the extension of `path` (.json, .txt, .md, .html) and is JSON for any other; a look at how the command line is used would settle it.
  - Guess: A result document can be passed in as `results` instead of naming a target, so a run on files can be exported without being kept; settled once the run on compiled fonts keeps its results.
  - Guess: The Markdown and HTML reports end with the checks that were skipped or failed to run, with their reasons; the reference tools' reports would settle whether to list them.
- [ ] `audit.checkOutlines` (M12, docs, live): Check Outlines
  - Guess: A fix of a file given by path is refused; the command line opens the source, fixes it and saves it, so the engine command only fixes the open document.
- [ ] `audit.checkInterpolation` (M12, docs, live): Check Interpolation
  - Guess: `showAll` lists the glyphs without problems in `glyphsWithoutProblems` instead of one line per glyph as the reference prints; its JSON output would settle the shape.
- [ ] `audit.compareFamily` (M12, docs, live): Check Family
  - Guess: The label is Check Family and the results go to the Font Audit panel, never to the Compare Family window, so the two features with similar names stay apart; the id stays for the command line (21-qa.md question 13).
- [ ] `audit.report.metrics` (M12, docs, live): Font Metrics Report
  - Guess: The underline thickness, which comparefamily does not print, is listed after the underline position and sorted the same way; the row's summary asks for it.
- [ ] `audit.report.panose` (M12, docs, live): PANOSE Report
  - Guess: Two misspelled value names of comparefamily's tables are spelled correctly (Borders, Literals); a reader comparing text output would notice the difference.
- [ ] `audit.showPanel` (M12, inferred): Font Audit
  - Guess: The Font Audit panel opens from the Window menu with no shortcut, because the reference app has no audit panel; a later decision on shortcuts would settle it (21-qa.md question 1).
- [ ] `audit.nextIssue` (M12, inferred): Next Issue
  - Guess: No menu item and no shortcut: the panel's arrow buttons and the Edit View marker badges run it, because the reference app has no audit panel (21-qa.md question 1).
- [ ] `audit.previousIssue` (M12, inferred): Previous Issue
  - Guess: No menu item and no shortcut: the panel's arrow buttons and the Edit View marker badges run it, because the reference app has no audit panel (21-qa.md question 1).
